L37: ## 1 Executive Summary
L38: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L39: Mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx is a folate receptor-alpha (FR\alpha)- directed antibody-drug conjugate (ADC). It contains a chimeric IgG1 directed against FR\alpha, a cleavable linker, and a DM4 payload. DM4 is a microtubule-disrupting agent. This product is not approved for any indication worldwide.
L40: The Applicant proposed the following indication for BLA 761310:
L41: *[TRADENAME] is indicated for the treatment of adult patients with folate receptor-alpha (FR\alpha) positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer, who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. Select patients for therapy based on an FDA-approved test.*
L42: *This indication is approved under accelerated approval based on tumor response rate and duration of response. Continued approval for this indication may be contingent upon verification and description of clinical benefit in a confirmatory trial.*
L43: The recommended indication for accelerated approval is:
L44: ELAHERE™ is indicated for the treatment of adult patients with folate receptor-alpha (FR\alpha) positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer, who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. Select patients for therapy based on an FDA-approved test.
L45: *This indication is approved under accelerated approval based on tumor response rate and duration of response. Continued approval for this indication may be contingent upon verification and description of clinical benefit in a confirmatory trial.*
L46: The recommended dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine is 6 mg/kg adjusted ideal body weight (AIBW) administered once every 3 weeks (21-day cycle) as an intravenous infusion until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L47: The total dose of ELAHERE is calculated based on each patient's AIBW using the following formula:
L48: \text{AIBW} = \text{Ideal Body Weight (IBW [kg])} + 0.4 \times (\text{Actual weight [kg]} - \text{IBW})\text{Female IBW (kg)} = 0.9 \times \text{height(cm)} - 92
L49: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L50: The review team recommends accelerated approval of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx for treatment of patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer, who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens, in accordance with 21 Code of Federal Regulations (CFR) Part 601.41, Subpart E for Biological Products Licensing Regulations. This recommendation is primarily based on efficacy and safety data from Study 0417 (SORAYA, NCT04296890), a single arm trial in 106 patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who had received one to three prior systemic regimens. To be eligible, patients had to have tumor tissue with “high” expression for FR\alpha, which was determined by the VENTANA FOLR1 (FOLR1-2.1) RxDx Assay. All patients were also required to and did receive prior bevacizumab. Patients received mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx 6 mg/kg (based on AIBW) as an intravenous infusion every 3 weeks until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. Tumor response assessments occurred every 6 weeks for the first 36 weeks and every 12 weeks thereafter.
L51: The major efficacy outcome measures were confirmed overall response rate (ORR) as assessed by investigator according to response evaluation criteria in solid tumors (RECIST) v1.1 and duration of response (DOR). The efficacy evaluable population included 104 patients with platinum-resistant disease, who had measurable disease, and received at least one dose of ELAHERE. Confirmed ORR by investigator was 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6). Median DOR was 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7), with a 4.8% complete response rate and a 26.9% partial response rate. Response assessment results using independent radiology review were consistent with investigator assessment.
L52: Available therapies in this treatment setting are limited to single agent chemotherapies with ORRs ranging from 6-27%. However, these data were collected prior to establishment of a platinum-based doublet \pm bevacizumab followed by maintenance treatment with bevacizumab and/or a poly(ADP-ribose) polymerase (PARP) inhibitor as standard of care for advanced ovarian cancer. More contemporary data suggest available chemotherapies have ORRs of 12-13% and median DORs of 4-7.4 months, and these data come from clinical trials where patients were less heavily pre-treated than those patients enrolled to Study 0417. It is also notable that the patient population enrolled into Study 0417 was a biomarker-defined population with FR\alpha-expressing tumors. To date, there are no other available therapies for patients with ovarian cancer which target this biomarker.
L53: Mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx demonstrated acceptable tolerability for the indicated population with a serious and life-threatening disease. The most common adverse reactions (\ge20 %) including laboratory abnormalities, were vision impairment, fatigue, increased aspartate aminotransferase, nausea, increased alanine aminotransferase, keratopathy, abdominal pain, decreased lymphocytes, peripheral neuropathy, diarrhea, decreased albumin,
L54: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L55: constipation, increased alkaline phosphatase, dry eye, decreased magnesium, decreased leukocytes, decreased neutrophils, and decreased hemoglobin. Serious adverse reactions occurred in 31% of patients receiving mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx, including intestinal obstruction (8%), ascites (4%), infection (3%), and pleural effusion (3%). Fatalities due to adverse reactions occurred in 2% of patients including small intestinal obstruction (1%) and pneumonitis (1%).
L56: Ocular toxicity is an important safety signal related to mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx. Ocular adverse reactions occurred in 61% of patients with ovarian cancer treated with mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx. Nine percent (9%) of patients experienced Grade 3 ocular adverse reactions, including visual impairment, keratopathy/keratitis, dry eye, photophobia, and eye pain; and one patient (0.2%) experienced Grade 4 keratopathy. The prescribing information recommends premedication and use of lubricating and ophthalmic topical steroids eye drops during treatment with mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx. The prescribing information also includes a Boxed Warning to advise health professionals of the risk of ocular toxicity. FDA will issue a postmarketing requirement to further characterize the incidence and severity of mirvetuximab soravtansine-related ocular adverse events and evaluate additional risk mitigation strategies for ocular adverse events.
L57: There was limited dosage exploration for mirvetuximab soravtansine, and the product was associated with a high frequency of ocular toxicity. A different dosing schedule may have a more optimal benefit-risk profile. FDA will issue a dose-finding postmarketing requirement for evaluation of alternate dosing schedules.
L58: A Pre-Market Approval (PMA) Application (PMA P220006) was submitted to CDRH for the Ventana FOLR1 (FOLR-2.1) RxDx Assay. The PMA will receive contemporaneous approval with the current BLA as a companion diagnostic device to identify patients with platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer for treatment with mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx.
L59: Study 0416 (MIRASOL) is an ongoing randomized Phase 3 trial comparing mirvetuximab soravtansine to investigator's choice chemotherapy in patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant, epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who have received 1-3 prior lines of systemic therapy. FDA requested blinded topline interim ORR/DOR data from Study 0416. These ORR/DOR data were supportive of the results from Study 0417.
L60: The results of this study will serve to confirm the benefit of mirvetuximab soravtansine for this indication.
L61: Overall, the benefit-risk profile based upon results from Study 0417 was favorable. Based on review of this application, as well as the current therapeutic landscape, the review team concludes that mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx represents a new treatment option for patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal
L62: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L63: cancer, who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. The ORR of 31.7% with a median duration of 6.9 is a surrogate endpoint that is reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit (i.e., improved survival or progression free survival) in patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who had received one to three prior systemic regimens cancer. This patient population has a significant unmet medical need for better treatment options.
L64: Therefore, the FDA review team recommends granting accelerated approval to mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx for the following indication:
L65: *ELAHERE is a folate receptor \alpha (FR\alpha)-directed antibody and microtubule inhibitor conjugate indicated for treatment of adult patients with FR\alpha positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer, who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. Select patients for therapy based upon an FDA-approved test.*
L66: *This indication is approved under accelerated approval based on tumor response rate and duration of response. Continued approval for this indication may be contingent upon verification and description of clinical benefit in a confirmatory trial.*
L67: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment
L68: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L69: Ovarian cancer, including fallopian tube and primary peritoneal cancer, is the fifth leading cause of cancer-related death for women in the United States. There will be an estimated 19,880 new cases of ovarian cancer and 12,810 deaths from ovarian cancer in the U.S. in 2022. Unfortunately, there is no recommended screening for ovarian cancer for the general population, and ovarian cancer is often diagnosed at an advanced stage. Five-year overall survival (OS) is only 49%.
L70: Initial treatment for patients with advanced ovarian cancer is typically surgery and (neo)adjuvant chemotherapy with a platinum-based doublet \pm bevacizumab. Patients who experience a complete response (CR) or partial response (PR) may be candidates for maintenance treatment with bevacizumab and/or a PARP inhibitor. Although most advanced ovarian cancer is initially platinum sensitive, virtually all patients ultimately develop platinum-resistant disease which is associated with a limited overall survival and few treatment options.
L71: Patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer who have received only 1-2 prior lines of systemic treatment and who have not previously received bevacizumab may be candidates to receive bevacizumab in combination with paclitaxel, topotecan, or pegylated liposomal doxorubicin (PLD). If patients cannot receive this regimen, or after progression of disease on this regimen, treatment options are limited to single agent chemotherapy with paclitaxel, topotecan, PLD, gemcitabine, or other options. Historical data suggest that single agent chemotherapies have ORRs ranging from 6-27%. However, these data were collected prior to establishment of a platinum-based doublet \pm bevacizumab followed by maintenance treatment with bevacizumab and/or a PARP inhibitor as standard of care for advanced ovarian cancer. More contemporary data suggest available chemotherapies have ORRs of 12-13% and median DORs of 4-7.4 months. In addition, a unique aspect of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx is that it targets FR\alpha, and its activity is enhanced in tumors with higher levels of FR\alpha expression. To this end, there are currently no other available therapies for ovarian cancer which target tumors overexpressing FR\alpha.
L72: The efficacy and safety assessment for mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx was primarily based on results from SORAYA, a single-arm international trial which enrolled 106 patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer. Patients had received 1-3 prior lines of therapy and all patients had received
L73: prior bevacizumab. Patients were selected for treatment if their tumors were positive for FR\alpha expression according to the Ventana FOLR1 (FOLR1-2.1) CDx assay. Patients received mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx 6 mg/kg intravenously every 3 weeks until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. Tumor response assessments occurred every 6 weeks for the first 36 weeks and every 12 weeks thereafter. The primary endpoint was confirmed ORR assessed by investigator according to RECIST v1.1. DOR was a secondary endpoint.
L74: There were 104 patients in the efficacy evaluable population for SORAYA. Study 0417 enrolled 96% White patients, 2% Asian patients, and 2% of patients with race not reported. Only 2% of patients were Hispanic or Latino. The patient population was not representative of a U.S.-based population with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer, with respect to race and ethnicity. Because of this, FDA issued a post-marketing commitment (PMC) for the Applicant to characterize the safety and efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx in patients from racial and ethnic minority groups. Regarding baseline disease characteristics and prior therapies, the Study 0417 population was representative of a U.S.-based population with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer. All patients had high-grade serous cancer, and 51% of patients had visceral metastases. Ten percent of patients had received 1 prior line of systemic therapy, 39% of patients had received 2 prior lines of systemic therapy, and 50% of patients had received 3 prior lines of systemic therapy. Thirty-eight percent of patients had received prior treatment in the platinum-resistant setting. All patients had received prior bevacizumab, and 49% of patients had received a PARP inhibitor.
L75: Confirmed ORR assessed by investigator according to RECIST v1.1 was 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6) and median DOR was 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7). Confirmed ORR and DOR assessed by BICR were generally consistent with the results per investigator. In addition, FDA requested blinded topline ORR/DOR data from Study 0416, an ongoing randomized trial in a similar treatment setting to Study 0417. Study 0416 is fully enrolled. These ORR/DOR data were supportive of the results from Study 0417.
L76: The safety profile of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx is generally reflective of the maytansinoid DM4 payload, which is an antimitotic agent that inhibits tubulin polymerization and microtubule assembly, resulting in cell cycle arrest and apoptosis. The main target organs for mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx-associated toxicity include the eyes, lungs, and peripheral nerves. Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 2% of patients of the 106 patients that made up the safety population on Study 0417 and included pneumonitis and small intestinal obstruction. Serious adverse reactions occurred in 31% of patients and included intestinal obstruction (8%), ascites (4%), infection (3%), and pleural effusion (3%). The most common adverse reactions (\ge20%) included vision impairment, fatigue, transaminase elevation, keratopathy,
L77: abdominal pain, decreased lymphocytes, peripheral neuropathy, diarrhea, decreased albumin, constipation, increased alkaline phosphatase, dry eye, decreased magnesium, decreased leukocytes, decreased neutrophils, and decreased hemoglobin. Adverse reactions leading to dose discontinuation occurred in 11% of patients, adverse reactions leading to dose interruptions occurred in 39% of patients and adverse reactions leading to dose reduction occurred in 20% of patients.
L78: Ocular toxicity is an important safety signal associated with mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx and affected 61% of patients enrolled to Study 0417, including 9% of patients experiencing grade 3 ocular adverse reactions and one patient who experienced grade 4 keratopathy. There is a Boxed Warning for ocular toxicity in the mirvetuximab soravtansine USPI to alert providers to the risk of developing severe ocular toxicities, including visual impairment, keratopathy, dry eye, photophobia, eye pain, and uveitis. Patients should adhere to the premedication and required eye care plan outlined in the USPI, and should be promptly referred to an eye care professional for any new or worsening ocular symptoms.
L79: Other important safety signals associated with mirvetuximab soravtansine include pneumonitis, peripheral neuropathy, and risk of embryo-fetal toxicity, which are listed under Warnings and Precautions in the USPI.
L80: Patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer, particularly those who have received prior bevacizumab, have limited treatment options consisting of single agent chemotherapies. Contemporary data suggesting these therapies have ORRs of 12-13% and median DORs of 4-7.4 months. Considering the limited therapeutic options for patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer who have received bevacizumab, along with the results from Study 0417, and ORR/DOR data from Study 0416, the review team considers that the efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine represents a meaningful advantage compared to available therapies, and these results are reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit. Safety and tolerability of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx were acceptable for a patient population with a serious and life-threatening illness and limited treatment options. Adverse reactions are manageable through pretreatment, supportive care, and labeling, including a Boxed Warning for ocular toxicity.
L81: Overall, the benefit-risk assessment for mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx is favorable. There is a post-marketing requirement (PMR) to submit results from the ongoing randomized trial, Study 0416, for confirmation of clinical benefit. This trial is comparing mirvetuximab soravtansine to investigator's choice chemotherapy in patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant, epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who have received 1-3 prior lines of
L82: systemic therapy.
L83: Patients should be selected for treatment with mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx according to the Ventana FOLR1 (FOLR-2.1) RxDx Assay. The assay is an Immunohistochemistry (IHC) assay indicated to select patients with epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer whose tumor cells display membrane staining for the folate receptor alpha (FOLR1) at moderate and/or strong intensity levels (\ge 75\% viable tumor cells with membrane staining). The PMA P220006 for the VENTANA FOLR1 (FOLR-2.1) RxDx Assay will be contemporaneously approved by CDRH with the BLA for mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx.
L84: Mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx is recommended for accelerated approval as follows:
L85: Mirvetuximab soravtansine is a folate receptor alpha (FR\alpha)-directed antibody and microtubule inhibitor conjugate indicated for the treatment of adult patients with FR\alpha positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer, who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. Select patients for therapy based on an FDA-approved test.

L95: ## 2 Therapeutic Context
L96: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L97: Ovarian cancer is the fifth leading cause of cancer-related death for women in the United States. In 2022, there will be an estimated 19,880 new cases of ovarian cancer and 12,810 deaths from ovarian cancer. The most common type of ovarian cancer is epithelial ovarian cancer, which comprises approximately 90% of all cases. Most epithelial ovarian cancers are high-grade serous tumors which are associated with a poorer prognosis compared to other ovarian cancer subtypes. There are no recommended screening measures for ovarian cancer for the general population, and unfortunately, ovarian cancer is often detected at an advanced stage. Ovarian cancer is associated with only a 49% five-year overall survival (American Cancer Society 2022).
L98: FR\alpha is an isoform of the FR which is normally expressed in epithelial cells including the kidney, retina, choroid plexus, and placenta. FR\alpha may be expressed in &gt;80% of serous ovarian cancers. Data are conflicting regarding the association between FR\alpha expression and tumor stage and grade. Although it is hypothesized that FR\alpha could mediate tumor progression, it is also unclear if FR\alpha expression is associated with prognosis for ovarian cancer. FR\alpha expression appears to be conserved with tumor progression (Vergote 2015).
L99: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L100: Initial treatment for patients with advanced ovarian cancer is typically hysterectomy/bilateral salphingo-oophorectomy with comprehensive staging and debulking. Patients then receive adjuvant chemotherapy with a platinum-based doublet. Some patients with bulky disease who are less likely to experience an optimal cytoreduction may receive neoadjuvant chemotherapy first with a platinum-based doublet \pm bevacizumab, followed by an interval debulking surgery with completion hysterectomy/BSO and cytoreduction, followed by adjuvant treatment with a platinum-based doublet \pm bevacizumab (NCCN 2022). Patients who experience a CR or PR (70-80% of patients) may be candidates to receive maintenance treatment with bevacizumab (if received with platinum-based chemotherapy), niraparib, olaparib, or olaparib + bevacizumab (Kemp 2013).
L101: Most patients will experience recurrence of disease. Patients with platinum-sensitive disease can receive treatment with a platinum-based doublet (\pm bevacizumab) and if they experience a CR or PR, they may be candidates for maintenance treatment with bevacizumab (if received with platinum-based chemotherapy), niraparib, olaparib, or rucaparib.
L102: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L103: Unfortunately, ultimately, almost all patients with advanced ovarian cancer develop platinum-resistant disease, which has limited survival and few treatment options. Patients who have received only 1-2 prior lines of systemic treatment and who have not previously received bevacizumab may be candidates to receive bevacizumab in combination with chemotherapy, either paclitaxel, topotecan, or pegylated liposomal doxorubicin (PLD) (AVASTIN USPI 2022). If patients cannot receive this regimen or if patients experience progression of disease (PD) on this regimen and are still candidates for systemic therapy, their treatment options are limited to single agent chemotherapy with paclitaxel, topotecan, PLD, gemcitabine, or other options.
L104: There are no therapies approved specifically for patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer who have already received a bevacizumab-containing regimen. There are also no therapies approved specifically for patients with FR\alpha-positive ovarian cancer. Potential treatment options for patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer who have already received a bevacizumab-containing regimen are summarized in Table 2.

L112: *PROC: platinum-resistant ovarian cancer
L113: **Reviewer comment:** It is challenging to evaluate the efficacy data for available therapies. Trials of the single agent chemotherapies occurred prior to adoption of the current treatment paradigm for advanced ovarian cancer which includes a platinum-based doublet \pm bevacizumab followed by maintenance treatment with bevacizumab or a PARP inhibitor. The efficacy results (ORR/DOR) in the table may not represent what patients would experience in the current era (2022) of treatment for advanced ovarian cancer.
L114: Data from the control arms for more recent trials is shown in Table 2 to get a sense of the efficacy which could be expected with these single agent chemotherapy options in the current era of treatment. It is important to note that even these trials are not reflective of the patients enrolled to Study 0417. In AURELIA, patients only received 1-2 prior lines of treatment, and no patients had received prior bevacizumab. In CORAIL, ~75% of patients had received 1-2 prior lines of treatment, and ~40% of patients had received prior bevacizumab. In NINJA, ~60% of patients had received 1-2 prior lines of treatment and it is unclear if patients had received prior bevacizumab. In Study 0403 (FORWARD 1), ~65% of patients had received 1-2 prior lines of treatment, and ~50% of patients had received prior bevacizumab. In general, generation of the efficacy results in the table occurred in less heavily pre-treated populations than those patients enrolled to Study 0417.

L124: ## 4 Significant Issues from Other Review Disciplines Pertinent to Clinical Conclusions on Efficacy and Safety
L125: ## 4.1. Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)
L126: DO1 consulted OSI to perform an audit of the Applicant as well as of the overall trial conduct. FDA selected three sites (Site 026: Dr. Jason Konner, Site 504: Dr. Domenica Lorusso, Site 932: Dr. Andrew Dean) for clinical inspection based on enrolling large numbers of patients in their respective geographic regions and/or having a higher ORR than observed on the trial. The trial sponsor, ImmunoGen, Inc., was also selected for inspection given that mirvetuximab soravtansine is an NME, and there have been no prior inspections of this sponsor. Based on these inspections, there were no significant regulatory violations, the trial conduct for Study 0417 appears adequate, and the submitted data from the three sites appear reliable.
L127: Clinical inspection summaries are included here:
L128: ## Site 025: Dr. Jason Konner
L129: Dr. Konner was inspected on July 18-22, 2022, as a surveillance and data audit for Study IMGN853-0417. This was the first FDA inspection of the investigator.
L130: The site screened 17 subjects and enrolled 4 into the study. As of the data cutoff date, 3 of the 4 subjects were discontinued from study treatment due to disease progression and one subject (# [REDACTED](b)(6)) remained on study treatment. At the time of this inspection, Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) was found to have been discontinued from study treatment due to disease progression. The study was active for survival follow-up but not open for new enrollment.
L131: Source records for all the enrolled subjects were reviewed and compared with the Applicant's submitted data for this site. Records reviewed during the inspection included, but were not limited to, the informed consent forms, eligibility information, shipping documentation of the tumor tissue or formalin-fixed, paraffin embedded (FFPE) slides submitted to the sponsor's designated central laboratory, study treatment received, protocol-required scans and related review reports and subsequent submissions to the central imaging laboratory, adverse events (AEs), concomitant medications, and protocol deviations. Regulatory documents related to the study administration and oversight at the site were also reviewed, including the Institutional Review Board (IRB) approvals of the protocol/amendments and related informed consents, site training for the protocol, delegation of authority log, FDA 1572s, financial disclosures, site's access to the electronic case report forms (eCRFs) system (i.e., Medidata RAVE), study monitoring activities, reports to the sponsor, control of investigation product from receipt to disposition, and retention of study records.
L132: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L133: The inspection found no regulatory violations at the site. All the enrolled subjects were found to have had their tumor specimens submitted to the sponsor's designated central laboratory [REDACTED] (b) (4) for evaluation of FR\alpha expression status. These subjects met the eligibility criteria for the study and received study treatment as scheduled until disease progression. Tumor assessments were conducted per protocol and the scans were reviewed and subsequently submitted to the sponsor's designated imaging lab [REDACTED] (b) (4) for central review. Results from the central review were found unavailable to the site.
L134: No Form FDA 483, Inspectional Observations, was issued to Dr. Konner at the conclusion of this inspection.
L135: ## **Site 504: Dr. Domenica Lorusso**
L136: Dr. Lorusso was inspected on July 18-22, 2022, as a surveillance and data audit for Study IMGN853-0417. For the investigator, this was the initial inspection by FDA. The site enrolled and treated 12 subjects in the study. As of the data cutoff date, 9 subjects were discontinued from study treatment due to disease progression and one subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) due to clinical deterioration, and two subjects (# [REDACTED] (b) (6)) continued receiving study treatment. At the time of this inspection, all the subjects were discontinued from study treatment. The study was closed to enrollment and continued to follow subjects for survival per protocol.
L137: Source records for all the 12 subjects were reviewed and compared relevant data with the submitted clinical data for this site. Records reviewed and confirmed in the inspection included the informed consents, eligibility criteria, premedication and treatment with the investigational product, study visits, RECIST assessments and documentation, adverse events, concomitant medications, protocol deviations, and data in the eCRFs. The inspection also reviewed regulatory documentation for the administration and oversight of this study at the site, including the local Ethics Committee approvals and communication related to the study protocol and amendments, prescreening consents for testing of tissue block(s) for FR\alpha expression, training activities and certifications, signed Form FDA 1572s, financial disclosures, access to the Medidata Rave system, control of the investigational product, study monitoring, and reports to the sponsor.
L138: The inspection revealed no significant regulatory deficiencies, with no Form FDA 483 issued to Dr. Lorusso. All the enrolled subjects were verified for their eligibility, study- required assessments, and reported clinical data, with no notable discrepancies identified except for two AEs (Grade 1 nausea and Grade 1 vomiting) for Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) which were documented in the progress note but not found in the eCRF or the submitted data listings for this subject. These two AEs occurred in [REDACTED] (b) (6) and were attributed to study treatment based on the clinical assessment. In addition, Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) was verified to have received an additional treatment with nab-paclitaxel plus relacoritant from [REDACTED] (b) (6) through [REDACTED] (b) (6) following disease progression on the third prior line treatment nab-paclitaxel alone from [REDACTED] (b) (6) to [REDACTED] (b) (6) while the subject participated in another clinical trial. The investigator acknowledged these findings, which were also discussed at the close of this inspection.
L139: **OSI Reviewer's Comments:** The unreported Grade 1 nausea and Grade 1 vomiting for Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) appear to be isolated since nausea and vomiting were reported for other subjects from the same site. In addition, both AEs were already included in the study report and the proposed label. It is less likely that the two unreported AEs can affect the safety profile of mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx in the study disease setting. For Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) the additional treatment with nab-paclitaxel plus relacoritant, as described above, should be deemed another line of prior therapy per protocol. This was reported as a protocol deviation in the study report. Given that relacoritant, a glucocorticoid receptor antagonist, was intended to overcome resistance to chemotherapy in an investigational manner, nab-paclitaxel appears to represent the backbone chemotherapy in this combination treatment.
L140: ## **Site 932: Dr. Andrew Dean**
L141: Dr. Dean was inspected on August 1-5, 2022, as a data audit for Study IMGN853-0417. This was the second FDA inspection of the investigator. The first one was conducted in August 2015 for another new drug application and was classified as No Action Indicated.
L142: For the current inspection, the established inspection report is not available as of 9/7/2022. Based on the inspector's preliminary summary, Dr. Dean's site enrolled 11 subjects into the study. All the 11 subjects received study treatment following enrollment. As of the data cutoff, 5 of the 11 subjects were discontinued from study treatment due to disease progression or adverse event(s) and the remaining 6 subjects continued receiving study treatment. At the time of this inspection, one subject died from disease progression and 10 subjects were on survival follow-up.
L143: The inspection audited source records for all the 11 subjects at the site and compared the Applicant's submitted data with source data. There were no discrepancies identified for subjects' eligibility and clinical data. Protocol-required efficacy assessments and investigator-reported tumor responses were verifiable with source data reviewed. There was no evidence of under-reporting of adverse events.
L144: No Form FDA 483 was issued to Dr. Dean at the conclusion of this inspection.

L146: ## **ImmunoGen, Inc.**

L151: ## 4.2. Product Quality
L152: The Office of Biotechnology Products, OPQ, CDER, recommended approval for ELAHERE (mirvetuximab soravtansine) manufactured by ImmunoGen, Inc. and found the data submitted were adequate to support the conclusion that the manufacture of ELAHERE is well-controlled and leads to a product that is pure and potent. The assessment of manufacturing information provided in the application and in the crossreferenced drug master file (DMF) concluded that the methodologies and processes used for the M9346A antibody intermediate, DM4 payload, sulfo-SPDB linker, drug substance, and drug product manufacturing, release and stability testing are robust and sufficiently controlled to result in a consistent and safe product. Additionally, the manufacturing site inspections were conducted, and the recommendation was for approval, based upon adequate inspection findings.
L153: There were several PMCs included in the review including to provide results for the stability indicating product quality attributes of the IMGN853DP shipped during the summer and winter months from the [REDACTED](b) (4) manufacturing site, to validate a gravimetric method to test the product, and to repeat the endotoxin method verification for in process samples of drug substance.

L157: ## 4.4. Devices and Companion Diagnostic Issues
L158: A Pre-Market Approval (PMA) Application (PMA P220006) was submitted to CDRH for the Ventana FOLR1 (FOLR-2.1) RxDx Assay.
L159: The Ventana FOLR1 (FOLR-2.1) RxDx Assay is a qualitative immunohistochemistry assay that can be used to assess folate receptor alpha (FOLR1) in formalin-fixed, paraffin-embedded epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer tissue specimens by light microscopy. The assay is for use with the OptiView DAB IHC Detection Kit for staining on a BenchMark ULTRA instrument.
L160: Effectiveness of the Ventana FOLR1 (FOLR-2.1) RxDx Assay was based upon patients enrolled to Study 0417. The eligibility for Study 0417 required that patients have tumors with positive FR\alpha expression. FR\alpha positivity was defined by the PS2+ scoring method, which is the methodology in which the pathologist describes the percentage of tumor cells expressing a certain intensity of membrane staining. The intensity of interest for the current study was determined to be levels of moderate (2+) or strong (3+), and this was scored by assessing the total number of viable tumor cells these staining levels. To be considered positive for FR\alpha expression, \ge 75\% of viable tumor cells in the specimen had to demonstrate FOLR1 membrane staining at moderate and/or strong staining intensity, as determined by the assay, which was interpreted at a central site.
L161: According to the CDRH team, the performance of the Ventana FOLR1 (FOLR1-2.1) RxDx Assay was supported by the analytical performance validation studies. It was determined that the data submitted in the PMA are supportive of approval of the Ventana FOLR1 (FOLR1-2.1) RxDx Assay as a companion diagnostic device to identify patients with epithelial ovarian, fallopian
L162: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L163: tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who may be eligible for treatment with mirvetuximab soravtansine.
L164: ## 5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L165: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L166: Mirvetuximab soravtansine (Elahere) is an antibody-drug conjugate (ADC) consisting of a humanized immunoglobulin G1 antibody (M9346A) directed against folate receptor alpha (FR\alpha), a succinimidyl-4-(pyridine-2-yl) disulfanyl-2-sulfo-butyrate (sulfo-SPDB) linker, and maytansine microtubule inhibitor, DM4. Each antibody is conjugated to an average of 3 to 4 molecules of DM4. Mirvetuximab soravtansine is administered by intravenous (IV) infusion once every 3 weeks at the recommended dose of 6 mg/kg (based on AIBW) for the treatment of adult patients with FR\alpha positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer, who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. The nonclinical development program for mirvetuximab soravtansine and the cytotoxic payload, DM4, consisted of studies in rats, monkeys, and rabbits to evaluate the primary and safety pharmacology, general toxicity, ocular toxicity, phototoxicity, and genotoxicity. During the nonclinical development, mirvetuximab soravtansine was referred to as IMGN853 or M9346A-sulfo-SPDB-DM4.
L167: Folate receptor alpha (FR\alpha, also known as FOLR1 or FR1) is an N-glycosylated protein anchored to the plasma membrane via glycosyl phosphatidylinositol with a molecular weight of 28 kD (Shen, et al., 1994). FR\alpha is upregulated on the surface of many epithelial-derived tumors, compared to normal tissue. This receptor plays a critical role in cellular uptake of folate, a basic component of cell metabolism and DNA synthesis and repair.
L168: In primary pharmacology studies, mirvetuximab soravtansine bound to FR\alpha expressed on human or Rhesus monkey cells but not endogenous mouse or rat FR\alpha. Binding to surface FR\alpha leads to internalization of the receptor-ADC complex and lysosomal degradation of mirvetuximab soravtansine. This degradation releases the DM4 from the antibody and can result in cell cycle arrest and cell death. In vivo, mirvetuximab soravtansine delayed tumor growth and caused tumor regression in several ovarian cancer xenograft mouse models when mice were administered a single IV dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine containing antibody doses as low as 1.2 mg/kg and DM4 dose of 25 \mug/kg. Based on the nonclinical pharmacology studies provided in the BLA submission, the established pharmacological class (EPC) of “folate receptor \alpha-directed antibody and microtubule inhibitor conjugate” is appropriate based on its pharmacological activity, prior EPC for related approved products, and the approved EPC for microtubule inhibitors.
L169: In vitro, DM4 was not stable in rat, human, and monkey plasma but was stable in human, rat, and monkey blood for up to 90 minutes. Metabolite S-methyl-DM4 (DM4-Me) was stable in human plasma either tested alone or in combination with DM4. In addition, S-methyl-DM4 was
L170: stable in human, rat, and monkey blood after incubation for up to 90 minutes. The binding of DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 to human plasma protein in vitro was 99.38% and 99.27%, respectively.
L171: In the 10-week GLP repeat-dose toxicity study in monkey, after a single IV dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine, the plasma exposure (C_{max} and AUC0-24) increased in an approximately dose proportional manner with no remarkable gender differences. The T_{1/2} values ranged from 95 to 106 hours on Day 1 and from 102 to 129 hours on Day 64. Upon repeated IV infusion of mirvetuximab soravtansine once every 3 weeks, no accumulation was observed. All monkeys administered mirvetuximab soravtansine at doses up to 8 mg/kg/dose tested negative for the presence of anti-drug antibodies (ADAs). Animal to human exposure margins were calculated using the mean clinical AUClast (15.91 h*mg/mL) for mirvetuximab soravtansine, C_{max} (143900 ng/mL) for mirvetuximab soravtansine, and C_{max} (4080 ng/mL) and AUClast (208.5 h*ng/mL) for DM4 after the first dose in patients receiving the recommended dose. Per the clinical pharmacology team, there was no accumulation with repeated dosing of mirvetuximab soravtansine; therefore, the steady state exposures after repeated dosing were similar to exposures seen after the first dose.
L172: In safety pharmacology studies, inhibition of hERG channels was not evaluated with mirvetuximab soravtansine; however, up to 60 \muM DM4 and metabolite S-methyl-DM4 had &lt; 50% inhibition on hERG potassium channels. Additionally, mirvetuximab soravtansine did not have effects on the nervous, cardiovascular, or respiratory systems in monkeys after repeated IV dosing up to 8 mg/kg (4-fold the exposures at the recommended dose based on AUC) based on ECG analysis and clinical observation.
L173: Following a single IV dose of 5 mg/kg radio-labeled mirvetuximab soravtansine to male Sprague Dawley rats, high radioactivity concentrations were observed in liver, bone marrow, lung, kidneys, and spleen. The eye:plasma ratio increased through the duration of the study, suggesting that drug clearance from the eyes was slower than from plasma. Radioactivity was also observed in testes suggesting that drug also penetrated the blood:testes barrier. The brain:plasma ratio was &lt; 3% of the plasma radioactivity of mirvetuximab soravtansine, indicating limited blood:brain barrier penetration. The major route of mirvetuximab soravtansine elimination was via feces in rats.
L174: A 10-week GLP compliant repeat-dose toxicity study with mirvetuximab soravtansine was conducted in cynomolgus monkey using the clinical IV route of administration, and the payload DM4 was assessed in cynomolgus monkey in a non-GLP single IV dose toxicity study. Administration of mirvetuximab soravtansine at 8 mg/kg for 10 weeks resulted in bone marrow toxicity with corresponding decreases in absolute neutrophils, white blood cells, and reticulocytes in both sexes when compared to control group. In addition, administration of mirvetuximab soravtansine at dose \ge 4 mg/kg resulted in ocular toxicity (minimal to marked degeneration/necrosis and attenuation in the corneal epithelium and minimal to slight epithelial degeneration/necrosis and inflammation of skin on the eyelids). Generally, the
L175: hematopoietic and ocular toxicity fully or partially recovered by the end of the 5-week recovery period. In humans, mirvetuximab soravtansine caused hematological toxicities, including pancytopenia (\ge 10\% Grade 2-4 decreased lymphocytes, hemoglobin, and neutrophils) at the recommended dose of 6 mg/kg IV once every 3 weeks. Mirvetuximab soravtansine also caused eye disorders (vision blurred, keratopathy, dry eye, cataract, photophobia, and eye pain) at the recommended dose. Thus, toxicological findings in the hematopoietic system and eyes in cynomolgus monkeys were consistent with the frequent adverse events observed in clinical trials.
L176: Mirvetuximab soravtansine at 5 to 10 mg/kg as a single dose and 8 mg/kg once every 3 weeks for 10 weeks resulted in skin toxicity, including slight to moderate epithelial ulceration/erosion, acanthosis, hyperkeratosis, and edema in both sexes (\ge 1-fold the exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC). Changes in skin were not observed at end of recovery period. In addition, DM4 showed phototoxicity with IC_{50} values of 0.582 \mug/mL and 8.712 \mug/mL in the presence and absence of UV light, respectively, in an *in vitro* Balb/c 3T3 mouse fibroblasts study. Mirvetuximab soravtansine showed positive staining of human skin in a tissue cross reactivity study; however, skin toxicity was not observed in clinical trials.
L177: Due to ocular toxicities observed in both animal toxicity studies and in patients, the Applicant also conducted GLP compliant repeated-dose toxicity studies to evaluate ocular toxicity of mirvetuximab soravtansine in male rabbits. Rabbits received up to 12 mg/kg/dose mirvetuximab soravtansine IV once weekly for 4 weeks or up to 10 mg/kg/dose once every 3 weeks for 12 weeks. A dose of 12 mg/kg once weekly resulted in early moribund sacrifice on Day 22 due to weight loss, anorexia, and skin lesions. Doses \ge 4 mg/kg weekly caused microscopic findings of attenuation and degeneration/necrosis of the corneal epithelium in the eyes of rabbits. These findings were still observed at the end of a 3-week recovery period. Similar microscopic findings in the eyes and skin were also observed in rabbits administered \ge 4 mg/kg mirvetuximab soravtansine once every 3 weeks; however, these findings were less severe when compared to once weekly dosing. Changes were still present following a 3-week recovery period but trending towards recovery.
L178: In a non-GLP single dose toxicity study of DM4, target organs of toxicity in cynomolgus monkeys included bone marrow (depletion), lung (hemorrhage) and inflammation at the site of injection. Microscopic findings in eye included mild mononuclear cell infiltration in ciliary body and minimal mononuclear cell infiltration in uvea. Similar histopathology findings in bone marrow and injection site were also observed in cynomolgus monkeys administered mirvetuximab soravtansine for 10-weeks.
L179: The Applicant submitted genotoxicity studies that evaluated the payload DM4 and its metabolite S-methyl-DM4. DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 were negative in the *in vitro* bacterial reverse mutation assay but positive in the *in vivo* rat bone marrow micronucleus assay. The genetic toxicity results with DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 are consistent with the mechanism of action and primary pharmacological activity. Carcinogenicity studies were not conducted with
L180: mirvetuximab soravtansine in accordance with ICH S9 guidance for advanced cancer indications.
L181: Dedicated reproductive toxicity studies were not conducted with mirvetuximab soravtansine in accordance with ICH S9 based on the expected adverse effects of DM4 on actively dividing cells and the intended indication for the treatment of patients with advanced cancer. Since mirvetuximab soravtansine targets actively dividing cells (e.g., adverse effects on the hematopoietic system in nonclinical studies and clinical trials) and is genotoxic, it is expected to cause teratogenicity and/or embryo-fetal death if administered to a pregnant woman. In addition, FR\alpha gene mutations have been associated with human birth defects, such as neural tube defects and neurologic disease (Trembath, et al., 1999; Cario, et al., 2009; and Steinfield, et al., 2009). Effective contraception use is recommended for females of reproductive potential during treatment with mirvetuximab soravtansine and for 7 months following the last dose. Since mirvetuximab soravtansine is clastogenic, a contraception period of 6 months with an additional 5 half-lives (mirvetuximab soravtansine half-life \sim5 days) is recommended to minimize the risk for DNA damage in oocytes, in accordance with FDA guidance *Oncology Pharmaceuticals: Reproductive Toxicity Testing and Labeling Recommendations*. Additionally, effective contraception use is recommended for male patients with female partners of reproductive potential during treatment and for 4 months after the last dose (3 months with an additional 5 half-lives) based on the FDA guidance. Due to the current ovarian cancer indication, the Applicant and review team agreed not to include a contraception recommendation for males in the prescribing information. In addition, mirvetuximab soravtansine has the potential to cause adverse reactions in a breastfed child. Based on the plasma half-life of \sim5 days in patients, the majority of mirvetuximab soravtansine should be cleared after 25 days; thus, women are advised not to breastfeed during treatment and for 1 month after the last dose.
L182: ## Recommendation
L183: The submitted nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology data with mirvetuximab soravtansine are adequate to support approval of Elahere for the proposed indication.
L184: ## 5.2. **Referenced NDAs, BLAs, DMFs**
L185: None.
L186: ## 5.3. **Pharmacology**
L187: ## Primary pharmacology
L188: Results of primary pharmacology of mirvetuximab soravtansine (IMGN853) were reviewed primarily under IND 111915 and additional relevant pharmacology studies are also reviewed and summarized below.
L189: Mirvetuximab soravtansine bound folate receptor 1 (FR1 or FR\alpha) expressed on human (K_d = 0.05-0.1 nM) or Rhesus monkey cells (K_d = 0.1 nM) but not endogenous mouse or rat folate receptor when determined by flow cytometry analysis (Study no. IMV28-004). When analyzed by an ELISA method, mirvetuximab soravtansine bound to FR\alpha expressed on human and cynomolgus monkey with a similar affinity (K_d = 0.2 nM) (Study no. IMV28-006). Amino acid sequence comparison showed high similarity (98%) between human FR\alpha and Rhesus monkey FR\alpha. Rabbit, dog, mouse, and rat sequences showed 87-90% sequence similarity and 83-88% sequence identity (Study no. IMV28-004).
L190: The binding specificity of unconjugated antibody M9346A was analyzed by flow cytometry in murine cells transfected with human FR\alpha and compared with non-transfected murine cells. The results showed that M9346A bound to human FR\alpha expressing cells but not to non-transfected cells. Similarly, M9346A did not bind to a FR\alpha negative human multiple myeloma cell line (Study no. IMV28-001). When analyzed by flow cytometry, M9346A and mirvetuximab soravtansine also bound to endogenous FR\alpha-positive human SKOV-3 ovarian cancer cells with the same K_d value at 0.1 nM (Study no. IMV28-001). In addition, the binding affinity of M9346A and mirvetuximab soravtansine was assessed by ELISA where recombinant human FR\alpha-Fc protein (human FR\alpha fused to Fc moiety of murine IgG2a) was used as the antigen. The affinity value (K_d) was 0.05 nM for both M9346A and IMGN853 (Study no. IMV28-001). M9346A also showed no competitive binding with natural folate for binding to FR\alpha (Study no. IMV28-001). Using an ELISA assay, M9346A did not bind to human FR\beta, FR\gamma, or FR\delta recombinant protein (Study no. IMV28-010, Study IMV28-012).
L191: To further assess the mechanism of action, the Applicant evaluated catabolism and lysosomal processing of IMGN853 and unconjugated antibody M9346A (Study no. IMV28-008). In vitro, when KB cervical carcinoma cells expressing high levels of FR\alpha were incubated with ^3H-IMGN853 (M9346A-sulfo-SPDB-^3H]-DM4) for 22 hours, the major metabolites were lysine-N6-sulfo-SPDB-DM4, DM4, and S-methyl-DM4. The metabolites represented approximately 12% of the initial dose of ^3H-DM4. To evaluate whether IMGN853 is activated primarily through lysosomal degradation, the Applicant compared the rate for processing of ^3H-IMGN853 to its three catabolites to the catabolism rate of unconjugated ^3H-M9346A antibody. M9346A was labeled with the radioactive tracer, N-succinimidyl-[2,3, ^3H] propionate to allow tracking of the intact antibody and release of the free label after culture with KB cells for 22 hours. The amount of protein-free radioactivity (processed) and protein-associated radioactivity (un-processed) was assessed following acetone extraction and liquid scintillation counting. The percentage of processed ^3H-M9346A antibody and ^3H-IMGN853 was similar, 28% vs. 23%, respectively. The Applicant concluded that activation of IMGN853 was primarily through lysosomal degradation. Thus, binding to surface FR\alpha on cancer cell lines leads to internalization of the receptor-ADC complex, lysosomal degradation of IMGN853, and intracellular release of maytansinoids.
L192: Anti-tumor activity of IMGN853 was evaluated in several ovarian cancer xenograft mouse models OVCAR-3, Igrov-1, and OV-90 (Study no. IMP28-001, IMP28-002, and IMP28-003). Treatment with a single IV dose of IMGN853 resulted in either delays in tumor growth (21 days
L193: to 120 days compared to control) or partial or complete regression with % tumor growth inhibition ranging from 9%-78% compared to control group. Anti-tumor activity was noted with IMGN853 containing antibody doses as low as 1.2 mg/kg and DM4 dose of 25 µg/kg (Study no. IMP28-001, IMP28-002, IMP28-003).
L194: IMGN853 mediated antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity in the presence of natural killer cells (Study no. IMV28-002) but did not mediate complement dependent cytotoxicity (Study no. IMV28-005). Moreover, antibody-dependent cellular phagocytosis (ADCP) activity of IMGN853 was also evaluated by incubating human peripheral blood mononuclear cell (PBMC)-derived macrophages with 2 target tumor cell lines expressing FR\alpha on their cell surface (adenocarcinoma KB cell line and human ovarian carcinoma IGROV1 cell line) in the presence of either IMGN853 or M9346A antibody at various concentrations (6.67 \times 10^{-8} M to 6.67 \times 10^{-13} M) and incubated for 2 hours at 37°C in 5% CO_2. The results demonstrated that IMGN853 and M9346A antibody induced a concentration-dependent ADCP activity against 2 cell lines tested, suggesting that conjugation to DM4 did not impact the ADCP activity of the antibody. The EC_{50} values were only determined for M9346A antibody, which were 1.4 \times 10^{-11} M and 4.2 \times 10^{-11} M ADCP activity for IGROV1 and KB cells, respectively.
L195: IMGN853 bound to both human and cynomolgus monkey tissues by immunohistochemical analysis (Study no. IMH28-003). Significant positive membrane staining was observed in 17 unique structural components isolated from 12 human tissues (lung, kidney, breast, choroid plexus, fallopian tube, pancreas, parathyroid, pituitary, salivary gland, skin, spinal cord, and stomach). Nine monkey tissues also showed significant positive staining in the choroid plexus, fallopian tube, kidney, pancreas, salivary gland, parathyroid, pituitary, spinal cord but weak staining in the skin, lung, breast, and stomach. The similarity of binding in human and cynomolgus monkey tissues confirmed that the cynomolgus monkey was an appropriate model for toxicological evaluation (Study no. IMH28-003).
L196: ## Safety Pharmacology
L197: ## **Study Title/Number: Effects of DM4 and DM4-Me on cloned hERG potassium channels expressed in human embryonic kidney cells/ 150328.JNO**
L198: **Methods:** The effect of DM4 (10-60 µM) and S-methyl-DM4 (DM4-Me, 10-60 µM) on in vitro hERG current was evaluated by using stably transfected HEK293 cells and the whole cell patch clamp method.
L199: **Results:** The IC_{50} for the inhibitory effect of DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 on hERG potassium current was not calculated but was estimated to be greater than 60 µM. Therefore, DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 are considered low potency potassium channel blockers.

L205: Distribution	
Study Title/Number: A non-GLP disposition study of IMGN853 (M-sulfo-SPFB-^{3}\text{H}-DM4) following intravenous bolus injection in male Sprague Dawley rats/CVK28-002
Assess the distribution, pharmacokinetics, metabolism, and excretion following a single IV administration of 5 mg/kg IMGN853 (M9346A -sulfo-SPDB-^{3}\text{H}-DM4) to male Sprague Dawley rats (n =3-15/group).
T_{1/2} = 138-214 hr
Mean blood to plasma concentration ratios ranged from 0.657 to 0.846.
The tissues with the highest maximum observed radioactivity concentration (C_{\text{max}} > 9000 \text{ ng} equivalent M9346A-sulfo-SPDB-^{3}\text{H}-DM4/g) included liver, bone marrow, lung, kidneys, and spleen.
The eye:plasma ratio increased through the duration of the study from 0.0312 to 0.603, suggesting the clearance of radioactivity from the eyes was slower than from plasma.
Radioactivity concentrations observed in testes suggested that drug also penetrated the blood:testes barrier. The testes:plasma ratio increased through the duration of the study from 0.0375 to 0.241.
L206: -The brain:plasma ratio indicated less than 3% of the plasma radioactivity was in the brain.
-Fecal excretion was the observed predominant route of elimination, accounting for a mean of 62.3% of the administered dose through 336 hours post-dose. Urinary excretion accounted for a mean of 2.27% of elimination.

Study Title/Number: In vitro plasma protein binding and blood-to-plasma partitioning of DM4 and DM4-Me in rat, monkey, and human/CVG28-001
Plasma protein binding in rat, monkey, and human
-DM4 was not stable in human, rat, and monkey plasma. However, S-methyl-DM4 (DM4-Me) was stable in human plasma either tested alone or in combination with DM4 for up to 8 hours.
-DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 were stable in human, rat, and monkey blood following incubation for up to 90 minutes.
-The plasma protein binding of DM4 was 96-99% across species.
-The blood-to-plasma partitioning of DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 was determined for human, rat, and monkey following incubation for 60 minutes. Overall, the blood-to-plasma concentration ratio and percent associated with blood cells decreased with increased DM4 concentrations.
-For S-methyl-DM4, partitioning to blood cells decreased with increased S-methyl-DM4 concentrations in rat and monkey blood, but not in human blood.

Excretion
Study Title/Number: Evaluation of the clearance of the maytansinoid DM4 in cynomolgus monkeys/ IMK14-001
-T_{1/2} = 2 hr
-The plasma concentration of maytansinoid DM4 in monkeys was only detectable at 0.5 hours post-administration (\sim 0.56 \mug/mL).
-Following 7 hours post-administration, levels of DM4 were below the limit of quantitation (3.6 ng/mL).
L207: The in vivo pharmacokinetics of the maytansinoid DM4 was evaluated in cynomolgus monkeys given a single IV dose of free DM4 at 0.1 mg/kg (n=4).	
Metabolism	
Study Title/Number: In vivo metabolism of IMGN853 (M-sulfo-SPDB-3H-DM4) in male Sprague Dawley rats following intravenous bolus injection/ IMG28-001
The in vivo metabolism of IMGN853 was assessed to characterize free payload (DM4) and related metabolites in acid treated plasma, urine, and bile after a single IV injection of 5 mg/kg [3H]-IMGN853 (i.e., M-sulfo-SPDB-3H-DM4) in male Sprague Dawley rats (n = 3).	
-Total radioactivity excreted in bile and urine within 0-72 hr was 18.4% and 0.7%, respectively.
-In plasma, radioactivity in organic extract (i.e., non-protein fraction that contained free payload and other small-molecule metabolites) represented 1% of total radioactivity through 336 hours post-dose.

L252: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L253: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L254: GLP compliant single and repeated dose toxicity studies of IMGN853 and a non-GLP single dose toxicity study of DM4 in cynomolgus monkeys were reviewed previously under IND 111915 and summarized below.
L255: ## **Study title/ number: 10-week intravenous (slow push) toxicity and toxicokinetic study in cynomolgus monkeys with a 5-week recovery period/ CVT28-001**
L256: IMGN853 at doses of 0, 2, 4, and 8 mg/kg/dose was administered intravenously (slow push) to cynomolgus monkeys once every 3 weeks for up to 10 weeks (a total of 4 doses) with a 5-week recovery period (n=6/sex/group). No early mortalities occurred. Drug-related clinical signs of toxicity included broken skin, dry skin, discolored skin, scaly skin, sores, scabs, and/or scars noted mostly in monkeys given \ge 4 mg/kg/dose.
L257: IMGN853-related ophthalmoscopic findings included keratopathy (faint perilimbal corneal pigment migration into cornea, especially superiorly) of both eyes in 3/6 males and 3/6 females at 8 mg/kg/dose in Week 9. Corneal pigmentation was likely secondary to other corneal changes. At the end of recovery period, similar corneal lesions developed in the superior perilimbal cornea in one previously unaffected female at 8 mg/kg/dose. In addition, the lesions (corneal pigmentation) were unchanged in a second female and more advanced in both eyes in a male at 8 mg/kg/dose. Target organs of toxicity associated with IMGN853 in monkeys were hematopoietic system (decreases \ge 30% in absolute neutrophils, white blood cell, and reticulocytes when compared to control in both sexes at 8 mg/kg dose), injection sites (at 8 mg/kg/dose showed slight to moderate epithelial ulceration/erosion); skin/subcutis (areas other than injection sites in males at 8 mg/kg/dose showed slight to moderate epithelial ulceration/erosion, acanthosis, hyperkeratosis, and edema); cornea (at \ge 4 mg/kg/dose showed minimal to marked degeneration/necrosis, pigmentation, and attenuation in the corneal epithelium); and skin of the eyelids (at \ge 4
L258: mg/kg/dose showed minimal to slight epithelial degeneration/necrosis and inflammation). Hematology and histopathology findings were trending towards recovery at end of 5-week recovery period. Histopathology findings of major target organs of toxicity for male and female rats are presented in Table 4 and Table 5, respectively.
L259: However, there were histopathology findings observed during the recovery period only, including minimal axon degeneration in the sciatic nerve in 1/2 females at 8 mg/kg/dose and minimal to slight fibrosis observed in testis in males at \ge 2 mg/kg/dose IMGN853 in 1/2 males in each dose group.

L267: ## **Study title/number:** A single-dose intravenous (slow push) toxicity study of IMGN853 (M9346A-sulfo-SPDB-DM4) in cynomolgus monkeys/ WRT28-001
L268: Five cynomolgus monkeys/sex/group were administered a single dose of IMGN853 at 0, 1, 3, 5, or 10 mg/kg. On Day 5, 3 animals/sex/group were sacrificed. The remaining recovery group animals were sacrificed on Day 29. There were no unscheduled mortalities in this study. The skin was a major target organ based on clinical observations of reddened areas and desquamation and histopathology findings of moderate vasculitis and ulceration. These toxicities occurred before Day 14 and with greater frequency in animals in the 10 mg/kg dose group; however, they were also observed at the lowest dose of 1 mg/kg. The other target organs of toxicity included injection site (minimal to moderate inflammation, ulceration, edema, and hemorrhage), bone marrow (minimal to moderate bone marrow depletion), spleen (mild lymphoid depletion), lymph nodes (mild lymphoid depletion), kidney (minimal to mild mononuclear cell infiltration and calculus formation, along with mild changes in clinical chemistry including decreased urea nitrogen and phosphorus at the 10 mg/kg dose), and lung (mild pleural fibrosis in the lung). Histopathology findings in bone marrow, kidney, and lungs were still observed at end of recovery period with less severity. There were no positive findings for anti-IMGN853 or anti-DM4 antibody production in any animals.
L269: ## **Study title/number:** Single dose toxicity study of maytansinoid DM4 in cynomolgus monkeys/ CRT14-001
L270: Monkeys were IV administered DM4 (L-DM4) at a dose of 0.1 mg/kg (n = 2/sex/ group) on Day 1 and euthanized on Day 5 (1/sex/group) and Day 29 (1/sex/group). No eye examination was performed/reported. All animals survived to the end of the study. Target organs of DM4 in monkeys include bone marrow (moderate to severe erythroid and myeloid depletion), lung (mild alveolar hemorrhage, erythrophagocytosis, and pigment), and inflammation in several organs (minimal to mild mononuclear cell infiltration in stomach, esophagus, and site of injection). Microscopic findings in eye included minimal and mild mononuclear cell infiltrates in ciliary body and minimal mononuclear cell infiltration in uvea.

L320: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L321: Carcinogenicity studies were not needed to support the advanced cancer indication, consistent with ICH S9 guidance.
L322: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L323: No reproductive or developmental animal toxicity studies were conducted with mirvetuximab soravtansine. The cytotoxic component of ELAHERE, DM4, disrupts microtubule function, is genotoxic, and can be toxic to actively dividing cells, suggesting it has the potential to cause embryotoxicity and teratogenicity.

L371: ## Immunohistochemistry:
L372: ## Study Title/Number: Evaluation of FR\alpha in normal human eye FFPE samples by immunohistochemistry/IMH28-018
L373: **Methods:** Grade 1-2 eye adverse reactions (blurred vision, keratopathy, and dry eye) have been observed in some of the patients treated with mirvetuximab soravtansine in the Phase 1 clinical trial. To help understand whether these adverse reactions are target related, the Applicant evaluated FR\alpha expression in a panel of nine normal human eye fixed paraffin embedded (FFPE) samples. Multiple eye compartments present across all these samples including cornea, limbus, conjunctiva, iris, retina, sclera, choroid, optic nerve, and ciliary body were evaluated.
L374: ## Results:
L375: The results showed that FR\alpha was not present in eight of the eye structures studied, including cornea epithelia, limbal cells, conjunctiva, iris, retina epithelia, sclera, choroid, and optic nerve. However, FR\alpha positivity was found in the epithelial cells of ciliary body.
