L377: X
L378: Primary Reviewer
Wimolnut Manheng
L379: Supervisor
Tiffany K. Ricks
L380: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L381: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L382: Mirvetuximab soravtansine (ELAHERE) is an ADC consisting of a anti-FR\alpha monoclonal antibody (immunoglobulin G1 [IgG1]) attached via a cleavable linker to the cytotoxic maytansinoid payload, DM4. Upon release from the antibody, DM4 inhibits tubulin polymerization and microtubule assembly, resulting in cell cycle arrest and apoptosis. The proposed indication for mirvetuximab soravtansine is the treatment of adult patients with folate receptor-alpha (FR\alpha) positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer, who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens.
L383: In the pivotal phase 3 trial, the ORR INV per RECIST v1.1 was 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6). Median DOR was 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7). Results for ORR and DOR as assessed by BICR were generally consistent with results as assessed by INV, with an overall concordance of 86% for ORR assessment between INV and BICR.
L384: A major focus of this review was the appropriateness of the proposed dosage of 6 mg/kg adjusted ideal body weight (AIBW) administered every 3 weeks (Q3W). Dosages ranging from 0.15-7 mg/kg total body weight and 5 to 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W were investigated in the dose escalation study. The recommended dose (i.e., 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W) was identified as the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) and recommended phase 2 dose (RP2D) in the dose finding trial. The major safety concern for this drug is ocular toxicity. The rate of ocular AEs in patients treated with mirvetuximab soravtansine was 59%, including blurred vision (46%) and keratopathy (37%). In addition, 35% of patients did not have resolution of their ocular AEs at the last follow-up.
L385: Exposure-response relationship for efficacy (ORR) and safety (ocular AEs of Grade 2+ and peripheral neuropathy Grade 2+) were observed at the proposed 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W dosage. Exposure-efficacy analysis at the 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W dose level indicated that over the range of exposures achieved at this dose level, lower exposure (AUC) was correlated with lower ORR. However, there was no relationship between maximum plasma concentration (C_{max}) of mirvetuximab soravtansine and ORR. Additionally, there was a correlation between C_{max} and incidence of Grade2+ ocular toxicities. There was insufficient evaluation of the alternative dosing schedules (e.g., modified weekly dosing regimen) that maintains the overall exposure (ie., lowering C_{max}, which is correlated with ocular toxicities while maintaining the overall exposure, or AUC, that is associated with efficacy). Therefore, a dose finding PMR will be issued for the following reasons:
L386: - Dose exploration study was not robust and did not provide sufficient evidence that the optimal dosage has been identified to maximize the risk-benefit profile.
- Mirvetuximab soravtansine caused very high rate of ocular toxicity
L387: The review team also evaluated the dosing recommendations for patients with hepatic impairment (HI). The clinical data contained limited to no information on patients with moderate or severe HI. Population PK analysis indicated that patients with mild HI had similar exposures of DM4 and its metabolite, s-methyl DM4 compared to patients with normal hepatic function. Further, based on the available safety data, there was no apparent impact of hepatic impairment on the safety including ocular AEs at the recommended dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine. Therefore, no dose-modification may be necessary for patients with mild HI. With regards to moderate or severe HI, unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4 exposures are expected to increase in these patients. Additionally, liver metastasis is commonly reported in patients with ovarian cancer. Therefore, avoiding use of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with moderate or severe HI would deprive patients with poor baseline liver function (e.g., moderate HI). Therefore, a PMR will be issued to evaluate an appropriate starting dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
L388: ## 6.1.1. Recommendations
L389: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology recommends the approval of BLA 761208 from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized below:
L390: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal or supportive evidence of effectiveness	Primary evidence of the effectiveness of mirvetuximab soravtansine in the proposed indication is derived from Study 0417. The observed ORR by IRC (primary endpoint) was 32.4% (95% CI: 23.6- 42.2%). BORs included 5 patients (4.8%) with CR and 29 patients (27.6%) with PR. The median DOR was 5.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 7.7 months) by IRC.
The exposure-response analysis identified a correlation between exposure and probability of achieving ORR. The analysis was conducted with data primarily from one dose level and thus needs to be interpreted with caution.
General dosing instructions	The proposed recommended dosage of mirvetuximab soravtansine is 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W administered as an IV infusion.
Dosing in patient Subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	No dosage modification is recommended for patients with mild HI (NCI-ODWG criteria). Mirvetuximab soravtansine should be avoided in patients with moderate or severe HI.
No dosage modification is recommended for patients with mild to moderate renal impairment (CL_{cr} \ge 30 mL/min). There are no data in patients with severe renal impairment (CL_{cr} < 30 mL/min).
L391: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L392: No mirvetuximab soravtansine dosage modification is recommended when used concomitantly with CYP3A inhibitors. Patients will be closely monitored for adverse reactions (including ocular AEs) when used concomitantly with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors.

Labeling	The clinical pharmacology review team has specific content and formatting change recommendations for the product labeling. Key modifications to the label made by the FDA include the recommendations to add dosing instructions in Section 8.6, Section 8.7 and the format and content in Section 12.3 and 12.6.
Bridge between the to-be marketed and clinical trial formulations	During development, there were no changes to the formulation.
L393: PMR or
PMC	Key Issues to
be Addressed	Rationale	Key Considerations for
Design
Features
PMR	Dose Selection	
The Applicant's recommended dosing regimen of mirvetuximab soravtansine is 6 mg/kg based on adjusted ideal body weight (AIBW), every three weeks (Q3W) for the treatment of adult patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. Among 106 patients who received treatment ELAHERE 6 mg/kg Q3W in the pivotal Study (IMGN853-0417), ocular AEs of all grades occurred in 59% of patients.
The limited data obtained from the dose escalation study suggest that alternative dosing schedules; specifically, a fractionated dosing regimen where smaller doses are administered more frequently, could possibly lower the incidence of ocular adverse events. Given the high rates of ocular toxicity observed with the 6 mg/kg Q3W dose, and limited data available at alternative dosing
Conduct a randomized clinical trial of at least 2 dose schedules of mirvetuximab soravtansine to characterize the safety and efficacy of the recommended dosage of 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W and alternative dosing schedule(s) (i.e., modified weekly dosing regimen, where lower doses are administered at a weekly frequency, instead of every 3 weeks) for the treatment of adult patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. The study should evaluate safety parameters including ocular AEs (all grade, Grade \ge 2), peripheral neuropathy (all grade, Grade \ge 2), and other
L394: regimens, additional clinical safety and efficacy information for mirvetuximab soravtansine is needed to determine the appropriate dosing regimen that will minimize the serious risk of ocular toxicity without a clinically significant impact on efficacy.	adverse events of interest and efficacy. The study should evaluate efficacy parameters of the alternate dosing schedules. The study should also include sufficient clinical pharmacokinetic sampling to analyze the exposure-response relationship for efficacy and safety.
PMR	Hepatic impairment	Based on nonclinical data, DM4 and s-methyl DM4 are CYP3A substrates. Hepatic metabolism and/or excretion is expected to play a major role in the elimination of unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4 given that urinary elimination was negligible. Therefore, hepatic impairment may increase the exposure of unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4 and thereby increase the risk of drug toxicity. There is limited to no data in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment. Additional information is required to determine appropriate and safe dosing of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with moderate hepatic impairment.	Conduct an open-label, non-randomized, dose-escalation trial, with expansion in a sufficient number of patients, to determine an appropriate starting dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with moderate hepatic impairment, according to the National Cancer Institute Organ Dysfunction Working Group (NCI-ODWG) criteria, in the target patient population. Collect safety and pharmacokinetic information for mirvetuximab soravtansine, unconjugated DM4, and s-methyl DM4 from the trial and utilize this information to determine the appropriate starting dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine for this population.
L395: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L396: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L397: **Mechanism of Action:** Mirvetuxumab soravtransine-gynx is an antibody-drug conjugate (ADC) containing a chimeric IgG1 monoclonal antibody targeting folate receptor alpha (FR\alpha) and a cytotoxic payload (DM4), which is a microtubule inhibitor, attached to the antibody via a cleavable linker. Upon release from the antibody, DM4 disrupts the microtubule network within the cell, resulting in cell cycle arrest and apoptotic cell death.
L398: **Absorption:** Mirvetuxumab soravtransine-gynx is administered as an IV infusion. Peak mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx concentrations were observed near the end of intravenous infusion, while peak unconjugated DM4 concentrations were observed on the second day after administration of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx, and the peak S-methyl-DM4 concentrations were observed approximately 3 days after administration of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx. Accumulation of the mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx, DM4, and S-methyl-DM4 was minimal following repeat administration of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx.
L399: **Distribution:** The mean (\pmSD) steady state volume of distribution of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx was 2.63 (\pm2.98) L. Unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4 showed &gt;99% plasma protein binding. The blood:plasma ratio for DM4 in human blood was 0.58 – 1.85 with inversely proportional to the concentration. The blood:plasma ratio for s-methyl DM4 in human blood was 0.55 – 0.58.
L400: **Metabolism:** Unconjugated DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 undergo metabolism by CYP3A4. In human plasma, DM4 and S-methyl DM4 were identified as the main circulating metabolites, accounting for approximately 0.4% and 1.4% of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx AUCs, respectively.
L401: **Elimination:** Non-clinical studies suggest that biliary excretion is the primary route of elimination for DM4 and renal excretion exhibit minor involvement (0.7 %).
L402: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L403: ## General Dosing
L404: The recommended dosage for mirvetuximab soravtansine is 6 mg/kg via IV Q3W based on AIBW. This dosing regimen showed reasonable efficacy (ORR of 31.7% [95% CI: 22.9, 41.6]) with a generally acceptable tolerability profile in the intended patient population. However, there are significant concerns regarding the high risk of ocular toxicity at the 6 mg/kg Q3W AIBW dosing regimen.
L405: The FDA notes that among 106 patients who received treatment with ELAHERE at 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W in the pivotal study IMGN853-0417 with a median follow-up of 11.3 months, vision impairment (any grade) occurred in 54% of patients, including 8% Grade 3 with no Grade 4
L406: events reported. Thirty seven percent of patients had keratopathy (any grade) with 8% of patients had Grade 3 and 1 patient with Grade 4 events reported.
L407: Nearly 24% (25/106) of the patients with ocular AEs required at least one dose interruption/reduction/discontinuation. Nineteen (19%) of patients required at least one dose reduction due to blurred vision and 13% of the patients required at least one dose-reduction due to keratopathy. Additional details about the safety of mirvetuximab soravtansine are provided in 8.2.
L408: Due to lack of robust dose finding data and very high rate of ocular AEs, a PMR will be issued in order to identify an optimal dosage schedule of mirvetuximab soravtansine.
L409: ## Therapeutic Individualization
L410: No dose adjustments are proposed based on intrinsic or extrinsic factors. Based on population PK analyses, mirvetuximab soravtansine PK was not affected by mild or moderate renal impairment and mild hepatic impairment. Of note, only one patient with moderate hepatic impairment was included in the analysis. There were no patients with either severe renal impairment or severe hepatic impairment. In addition, intrinsic factors such as age and AIBW were identified to be significant covariates in population PK analysis. However, the influence of these covariates on the PK of mirvetuximab soravtansine was &lt; \pm 20\%. Nevertheless, AIBW was found to be a significant covariate on IMGN853 exposure and therefore, the recommended dosage is based on AIBW to lower the inter-subject variability. No dosage modifications for any other covariates was warranted. Dedicated drug-drug interactions (DDI) studies were not conducted with mirvetuximab soravtansine. In vitro studies suggest that mirvetuximab soravtansine may exhibit potential DDIs.
L411: There were insufficient number of non-white patients (91.7% White [n = 498], 2.2% Black [n = 12], 2.8% Asian [n = 15], 0.4% American Indian [n = 2], 2.9% Other or Not reported [n = 18]) in the clinical studies included in the application. The Applicant did not assess the impact of race and ethnicity in the population PK analysis. As outlined in the section below (clinical), a PMC will be issued to conduct an integrated analysis containing data from clinical trials and other data sources such as post-marketing reports, real-world evidence and other sources to further characterize the safety and efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients from racial and ethnic minority groups.
L412: ## Outstanding Issues
L413: FDA will issue two PMRs to address the outstanding issues based on the review of the clinical data submitted by the Applicant.
L414: - There was a limited dose-exploration with mirvetuximab soravtansine in the dose-escalation study (Study IMGN853-0401). The applicant selected the dose of 6 mg/kg
L415: AlBW Q3W for the pivotal efficacy study (IMGN853-0417). A significantly higher rate of ocular AEs was reported among all the patients treated at this dosage. Since the dose escalation study enrolled very few patients at dosage schedules other than 6 mg/kg Q3W, robust dose or exposure-response data were not available to enable optimal dose selection. Based on available data, it is difficult to determine whether 6 mg/kg AlBW Q3W of mirvetuximab soravtansine provides the optimal risk-benefit profile.
L416: The limited clinical data obtained from the dose-escalation study suggest that alternative dosing schedules; specifically, a fractionated dosing regimen where smaller doses are administered more frequently (i.e., 3X QW doses over 4 weeks), could possibly lower the incidence of ocular adverse events. Given the high rates of ocular toxicity observed with the 6 mg/kg AlBW Q3W dose, and limited data available at alternative dosing regimens, additional clinical safety and efficacy information for mirvetuximab soravtansine is needed to determine the optimal dosage that will minimize the serious risk of ocular toxicity without a clinically significant impact on efficacy. Therefore, the applicant will be asked to conduct a postmarketing dose finding study to assess the safety and efficacy of alternative dose schedules of mirvetuximab soravtansine.
L417: - The clinical pharmacology portions of the application contains data from only a limited number of patients with moderate (n = 1) or severe hepatic impairment. Based on nonclinical data, unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4 are CYP3A substrates. Therefore, hepatic metabolism and/or excretion is expected to play a major role in the elimination of unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4. Given that the hepatic impairment may increase the exposure of unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4 and thereby increase the risk of drug toxicity, additional information is required to determine appropriate and safe dosing of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
L418: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L419: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L420: PHARMACOLOGY
Structure	Mirvetuximab soravtansine consists of an anti-FR\alpha monoclonal antibody (immunoglobulin G1 [IgG1]) attached via a cleavable linker to the cytotoxic maytansinoid payload, DM4. Maytansinoids are anti-mitotic agents that inhibit tubulin polymerization and microtubule assembly, resulting in cell cycle arrest and apoptosis. Mirvetuximab soravtansine contains an average of 3.4 DM4 molecules bound to the antibody. The payload is known to form the active metabolite, S-methyl-DM4.
Mechanism of Action	The anticancer activity mirvetuximab soravtansine is due to the binding of the ADC to FR\alpha expressing cells, followed by internalization of the ADC. Once
L421: inside, the cytotoxic small molecule, DM4, is released via proteolytic cleavage. DM4 disrupts the microtubule network within the cell, resulting in cell cycle arrest and apoptotic cell death.

QT Prolongation	
In the dose-escalation/expansion phase 1 study (IMGN853-0401), no large changes in mean QTc intervals (>20 msec) were noted at the recommended dosage of mirvetuximab soravtansine.
Increases in QTcF of > 500 msec were not observed in the mirvetuximab soravtansine arm. Central tendency analysis of the QTcF interval changes from baseline showed that the highest mean (upper bound of the 2-sided 90% CI) for QTcF was 8.1 (4.1, 13.0) msec, which was observed at cycle 3/Day 1/end of infusion in the Expansion cohort 3 of mirvetuximab soravtansine arm.

GENERAL INFORMATION
Bioanalysis	
All Patients (Studies IMGN853-0401, IMGN853-0403, and IMGN853-0417)
Plasma concentrations of mirvetuximab soravtansine and total antibody were measured using a validated ELISA method.
A validated high performance liquid chromatography coupled to tandem mass spectrometry (MS/MS) detection was developed for the quantitation of DM4 and S-methyl DM4 analytes.
Immunogenicity
An ECL-based and a cell-based assay were used to detect ADA and nAb to mirvetuximab soravtansine in human plasma.

Dose Proportionality	
Following IV infusion, the exposures (C_{max} and AUC_{0-\text{INF}}) of ADC and total Ab showed dose-proportional increase at 3.3 to 7 mg/kg total BW. In the lower dose range from 0.15 to 2 mg/kg TBW, the clearance decreased with increasing dose, suggesting a saturable mechanism of elimination such as target-mediated drug disposition.

Accumulation	
There was minimal accumulation with repeated administrations.
Mean (%CV) Accumulation Ratios: ADC – 1.15 (22.4%); TAb – 1.41 (29.1%), DM4 - 1.1 and s-methyl DM4 - 1.01.

Variability	
Pharmacokinetic data obtained from the clinical studies show that Mirvetuximab soravtansine exhibits low to moderate variability. The %CV for AUC_{ss} and C_{MAX} predicted with PopPK model were 22.6% and 17.7%, respectively at the recommended dose.
Mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx Mean (\pmSD)	Unconjugated DM4 Mean (\pmSD)	S-methyl-DM4 Mean (\pmSD)
C_{max}	137.3 (\pm62.3) \mug/mL	4.11 (\pm2.29) ng/mL	6.98 (\pm6.79) ng/mL
AUC_{\tau}	20.65 (\pm6.84) h*mg/mL	530 (\pm245) h*ng/mL	1848 (\pm1585) h*ng/mL

Immunogenicity	
With a median duration of treatment of 4.3 months in Studies 0417, 0401, and 0403, a total 55/423 (13%) of mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx treated at
L422: 6 mg/kg AIBW in EOC patients had at least 1 post-baseline positive sample for anti-mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx antibodies. Of these, 28/423 patients (7%) had developed treatment-emergent ADA and 3/423 patients (<1%) had treatment-enhanced ADA. Neutralizing antibodies were detected in 24/423 (6%) of patients.

DISTRIBUTION
Volume of Distribution	
Based on population PK analysis, the mean volume of distribution (%CV): Cycle 1: 3.198 L (23.2%); Cycle 3: 3.172 L (32.3%) in Study IMGN853-0401. The steady state Vdss = 2.63 (\pm 2.98) L. Based on the small Vd, distribution may be primarily limited in plasma or in vascular rich tissues.

Plasma Protein Binding	
Unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4 were highly bound to plasma proteins (>99%).

Blood-to-Plasma ratio	
In preclinical studies, mirvetuximab soravtansine exhibited limited distribution to red blood cells in rats, monkeys, and humans. The blood-to-plasma ratio in human blood was inversely proportional to concentration and ranged between 0.58 – 1.85 for DM4. The blood-to-plasma ratio for s-methyl DM4 was 0.55 – 0.58 in human blood.

ELIMINATION
Mean Terminal Half-Life	
Following IV infusion at 6 mg/kg AIBW, the terminal elimination half-life was 115 h (4.8 days), indicating the steady state can be reached in approximately 24 days (in Cycle 2).

METABOLISM
Primary Metabolic Pathways	
Nonclinical studies using human biomaterials showed that CYP3A4 is the predominant CYP450 enzyme responsible for the biotransformation of DM4 and S-methyl-DM4.

Transporter Substrate	DM4 and S-methyl DM4 are substrates of the MDR1 but not inhibitors of MDR1.
DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 were substrates of P-pg, but neither was an inhibitor of this efflux transporter.

DDI Potential	DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 showed no direct inhibition on CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4/5.
DM4 showed time-dependent inhibition of CYP3A4, and S-methyl DM4 showed weak time-dependent inhibition of CYP2C8 and CYP2C9.
DM4 and S-methyl-DM4 did not induce CYP1A2, CYP2B6, or CYP3A4.
Dedicated DDI studies have not been conducted with mirvetuximab soravtansine.

EXCRETION
Primary Excretion Pathway	
In preclinical species, feces, via biliary elimination, is the main route of excretion following IV doses of mirvetuximab soravtansine. Urinary excretion accounting for approximately 0.7% of the dose. No mass-balance studies were conducted in human subjects.

L428: ## Dosing Regimen Selection:
L429: To support the proposed dosing regimen of 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W, the applicant rationalized based on the risk:benefit assessment.
L430: As shown in the Table 6 above, Study IMGN853-0401 evaluated several dose levels administered in a Q3W schedule. In addition, a smaller cohort of the patients were also treated with modified weekly dosing schedule (3 weekly doses over 4 weeks). However, there was limited clinical data collected at dose-levels and/or schedules other than the recommended dosage of 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W.
L431: Per the Applicant, based upon the totality of the safety, anti-tumor activity data, and PK, 6 mg/kg AIBW IV Q3W was declared the MTD/RP2D for the expansion cohorts. The use of the AIBW dosing at 6 mg/kg ensured maximum dose intensity with a tolerable safety profile. As
L432: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}

L446: Overall, the safety data from the dose escalation and expansion trials suggest that the modified weekly dosing regimen may improve the safety of ELAHERE. L447: ## **Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?**
L448: The data and information are insufficient to determine whether the proposed dosage of mirvetuximab soravtansine is appropriate. As detailed above, the 6 mg/kg dose was selected for the pivotal study evaluation based on the efficacy and safety data and the exposure-response analysis for efficacy and safety from the phase 1 trial. The pivotal trial IMGN853-0417 was an open-label, single arm study of mirvetuximab soravtansine in adult patients platinum-resistant high-grade serous epithelial ovarian, primary peritoneal, or fallopian tube cancer, whose tumors express a high-level of FR\alpha, and who have received prior bevacizumab
L449: treatment. The primary endpoint was ORR. The observed ORR rate in the pivotal study was 32.4% (95% CI: 23.6, 42.2 %) including 5 CR (4.8%) and 29 PR (27.6%).
L450: ## Exposure-response relationship
L451: Exposure-response (E-R) analyses for safety and efficacy were conducted using data collected from the pivotal trial (Study IMGN853-0417) at a dose of 6 mg/kg (n = 106) and the supportive phase 1 (Study IMGN853-0401) and phase 3 trial (Study IMGN853-0403) at doses ranging from 0.15 mg/kg to 7 mg/kg.
L452: Due to the differences in study design, the efficacy endpoints were analyzed in two separate data pools, one for the pivotal study (IMGN853-0417, N = 97), and the other for the supporting studies IMGN853-0401 (patients meeting the IMGN853-0403 inclusion criteria, N = 36) and IMGN853-0403 (N = 242) combined. The safety endpoints were analyzed with data from the three studies pooled together (N = 542) and a summary is shown in Table 9.

L457: ## Exposure-efficacy relationship
L458: The E-R analysis for efficacy identified a positive correlation between the AUC of mirvetuximab soravtansine in the first cycle (AUC_{0-21d}) and C_{trough}, and the probability of achieving ORR, shown in Figure 2. *The Applicant stated that there was no relationship between mirvetuximab soravtansine C_{max} and ORR.*
L459: **Figure 2: Observed Objective Response Rate vs. Mirvetuximab soravtansine C_{trough} and AUC_{0-21d} with Model Predicted Probability Overlay - IMGN853-0417**
L460: Source: Applicant's E-R analysis Report QPK28-007 – Figures 4 and A.3.4
L461: In stepwise model selection procedure, a forward and backward, Akaike information criterion (AIC) based, automatic selection process was used to identify important exposure relationships and covariates. Therefore, the Applicant opined that the C_{trough} and AUC_{0-21d} are more appropriate exposure parameters for the E-R analysis.
L462: It should be noted that the final E-R analysis for efficacy was conducted with clinical data obtained from Study IMGN853-0417 since the patients from the other studies (i.e., Study IMGN853-0401 and Study IMGN853-0403) were heterogeneous with regards to the FR\alpha expression levels.
L463: ## Exposure-safety relationship
L464: The E-R analysis for safety evaluated a relationship between the ELAHERE exposure and the key safety endpoints including ocular AEs of Grade \ge 2, peripheral neuropathy (PN), pneumonitis and interstitial lung disease (ILD). The analysis identified a positive correlation between the AUC_{0-21d} of mirvetuximab soravtansine and probability of ocular AEs of Grade \ge 2 or PN (Figure 3). As such, the incidence of PN was very low (including Grade \ge 2) and the E-R for PN showed a shallow relationship.
L465: **Figure 3: Observed Ocular AEs (Grade \ge 2) (Left) and Peripheral Neuropathy (Grade \ge 2) (Right) vs. Mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-21d with Model Predicted Probability Overlay**
L466: Source: Applicant's E-R analysis Report QPK28-007 – Figures 16 and 18
L467: In contrast to E-R for efficacy, there was a significant relationship between Cmax of ELAHERE and ocular AEs of Grade \ge 2, as shown in Figure 4.
L468: **Figure 4: Ocular AEs (Grade \ge 2) vs. Mirvetuximab Soravtansine Cmax**
L469: Source: Applicant's response to information request – Figure 11
L470: Based on the model predicted exposure parameters for ELAHERE (AUC0-21d, Cmax, and Ctrough), the modified weekly dosing regimens generally showed lower Cmax compared to the Q3W dosing regimens including 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W (Table 10). Since the clinical data from the modified weekly dosing schedule is available from limited patients (n = 25 across different dose levels), there is inadequate information to determine an ideal exposure parameter that would
L471: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L472: explain the relationship between the observed ocular AEs and ELAHERE exposure. It is noteworthy that ~95% of the pooled safety data that was included in the E-R analysis for safety events represent the exposure parameters at Q3W dosing regimens. Therefore, the E-R analysis for efficacy and safety should be interpreted with caution.

L476: Collectively, given the limitations noted above regarding the clinical data at alternative dosage schedules, additional clinical safety and efficacy information on mirvetuximab soravtansine is needed to determine a dosing regimen and schedule that may minimize the risk of ocular toxicity without a clinically significant impact on efficacy. The Applicant will be asked to conduct a post-marketing study to evaluate the alternative dosing schedules.
L477: ## **Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?**
L478: No, an alternative dosing regimen and management strategy is not required based on age and BW. Refer to Section 19.4 for a detailed discussion.
L479: ## **Renal Impairment**
L480: No dose modification is recommended in patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. No formal clinical trial has been conducted to assess the effect of renal function on the PK of mirvetuximab soravtansine. The effect of mild and moderate renal impairment on mirvetuximab soravtansine, unconjugated DM4, and s-methyl DM4 exposure was assessed using the population PK model. The population PK analysis included patients with mild (n=192)
L481: and moderate (n=111) renal impairment, in addition to patients with normal (n=175) renal function. There was no marked difference in model-predicted Cycle 1 mirvetuximab soravtansine, unconjugated DM4, and s-methyl DM4 exposures (AUC_{\tau} and C_{max}) between patients with normal renal function and those with mild and moderate renal impairment (Figure 5).
L482: **Figure 5: Predicted AUC_{\tau} (upper panel) and C_{max} (lower panel) at Cycle 1, by Renal Function**
L483: Source: Applicant's PopPK Report QPK28-006 – Figures 34 and 35
L484: The effect of severe renal impairment on the PK of mirvetuximab soravtansine, unconjugated DM4, and s-methyl DM4 has not been assessed, and this lack of information is conveyed in section 8.6 of product labeling.
L485: ## Hepatic impairment
L486: No dose modification is recommended in patients with mild hepatic impairment. No formal clinical trial has been conducted to assess the effect of hepatic function on the PK of mirvetuximab soravtansine. The effect of mild hepatic impairment on mirvetuximab soravtansine, free DM4 or s-methyl DM4 exposure was assessed using the population PK model. The population PK analysis included patients with mild (n=71) hepatic impairment, in
L487: addition to patients with normal (n=386) hepatic function. Model-predicted Cycle 1 ADC, DM4 or s-methyl DM4 exposures in patients with mild hepatic impairment were similar to patients with normal hepatic function (Figure 6). There was only 1 patient with moderate HI and 21 patients whose hepatic impairment status was missing. Given the limited number of patients with moderate and severe HI, the exposures in these patients are unknown.
L488: **Figure 6: Predicted AUC_{\tau} (upper panel) and C_{max} (lower panel) at Cycle 1, by Hepatic Function**
L489: Source: Applicant's PopPK Report QPK28-006 – Figures 37 and 38
L490: Comparison of safety by baseline hepatic function (per the NCI-ODWG criteria) across all patients who received mirvetuximab soravtansine at 6 mg/kg Q3W IV in studies IMGN-0401, IMGN-0403, and IMGN-0417 (Table 11) indicated no marked change in overall tolerability in mild hepatic impairment. However, patients with mild hepatic impairment had a numerically higher incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs and Grade \ge 3 SAEs.

L495: Given that there is no substantial decrease in the tolerability of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with mild hepatic impairment, no dose adjustment is recommended for this population.
L496: The effect of moderate or severe hepatic impairment on the PK and safety of mirvetuximab soravtansine has not been assessed. However, based on the known pathology of patients with ovarian cancer, hepatic involvement leading to impaired hepatic function may be commonly anticipated with the target patient population. The current version of the label Section 8.7 suggests avoiding use of mirvetuximab soravtansine use in patients with moderate or severe HI, which would limit the availability of the treatment to these patients. Therefore, a PMR will be issued to collect additional information to determine appropriate and safe dosing of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
L497: ## Folate Receptor alpha (FR\alpha) Expression:
L498: Eligibility for studies IMGN853-0401, IMGN853-0403, and IMGN853-0417 required patients to have specific tumor expression levels of folate receptor alpha (FR\alpha) as detected by the Ventana FOLR1 IHC assay. If a patient did not have archival material available for analysis, a fresh biopsy was obtained for enrollment. The IMGN853-0403 trial required minimum FR\alpha expression levels of \ge50% of tumor cells with any FR\alpha membrane staining visible at \le10X microscope magnification. Additionally, a stratification cutoff of \ge75% of tumor cells with any FR\alpha membrane staining visible at 10X magnification was also applied. Analyses of the IMGN853-
L499: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L500: 0403 trial revealed increased objective response rate (ORR) in the population of patients with \ge 75\% FR\alpha cutoff, as compared to patients in the ITT population with \ge 75.50\% FR\alpha expression, so this cutoff (\ge 75\%) was utilized in the subsequent IMGN853-0417 trial. The IMGN853-0417 trial utilized a PS2+ scoring method based on membrane stain intensity level of 2 or greater. To be considered positive for FR\alpha expression and eligibility for the trial, \ge 75\% of viable tumor cells must have exhibited level 2 and/or 3 membrane staining intensity. In addition to determining the FR\alpha expression levels for enrollment, the Applicant performed exploratory analyses investigating the effects of FCGR polymorphisms, BRCA mutations status, and soluble FR\alpha levels on the response of mirvetuximab soravtansine.
L501: To assess the appropriateness of the FR\alpha cutoff utilized by the Applicant to determine FR\alpha High (\ge 75\%) in 0403 trial and subsequent 0417 trial, the reviewer conducted exploratory analyses utilizing a more stringent 90% cutoff and assessing the relationship to efficacy within these trials. For trial 0403, the ORR in patients receiving MIRV was similar across FR\alpha expression levels but was numerically higher in patients taking MIRV with \ge 90\% FR\alpha expression (28%) compared to the ITT (22%), FR\alpha High (24%), and FR\alpha \ge 50\% but &lt; 75\% (20%) populations. In trial 0417, patients taking MIRV with FR\alpha expression \ge 90\% had slightly higher ORR (36%) than the FR\alpha High patients (32%). However, it should be noted, that while the ORR was slightly higher utilizing the FR\alpha \ge 90\% threshold, the number of patients that met this criterion was approximately 50% of the patients meeting the FR\alpha \ge 75\% cutoff (Table 12). As such, it appears that the FR\alpha \ge 75\% cutoff utilized by the Applicant for enrollment in the 0417 trial and for stratification into FR\alpha High in trial 0403 identifies a population of patients who are responsive to MIRV without excluding a large number of patients who may benefit.

L504: The Applicant also performed exploratory analyses related to the impact of FCGR genotype, BRCA mutation status, and soluble FR\alpha levels on the ORR of mirvetuximab soravtansine. In trial 0403, the impact on response was assessed for the FCGR3A F158V variant, which has previously been shown to impact ADCC activity. The analyses showed that patients with the F/F genotype
L505: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L506: had numerically higher ORR (31%) than patients with the V/V genotype (19%); however, this relationship was not statistically significant. The analyses for patients with mutations in either the BRCA1 or BRCA2 genes as well as analyses correlating soluble FR\alpha levels with response did not show a statistically significant impact on efficacy.
L507: In summary, exploratory analyses by the Applicant investigating the impact of FCGR polymorphisms, mutations in either the BRCA1 or BRCA2 genes, or soluble FR\alpha levels did not reveal statistically significant impacts on response to mirvetuximab soravtansine. In addition, exploratory analyses by the reviewer investigating the impact of a higher FR\alpha expression cutoff (\ge 90% vs \ge 75%) illustrates the proposed cutoff of \ge 75% FR\alpha expression is reasonable, and balances increased ORR in FR\alpha positive patients compared to the ITT population and the number of patients that meet the defined cutoff value.
L508: ## **Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?**
L509: As mirvetuximab soravtansine is administered intravenously, no clinical assessment of potential food-drug interactions has been performed.
L510: Dedicated clinical studies to assess potential drug-drug interactions (DDIs) have not been performed. As such, DDIs involving antibodies are not anticipated. Unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4 are substrates of CYP3A4 and therefore, concomitant use of ELAHERE with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors may increase unconjugated DM4 exposure and thereby AEs associated with ELAHERE. The proposed label Section 7.1 recommends close monitoring of patients for adverse reactions with ELAHERE when used concomitantly with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors. The applicant performed population PK analysis to evaluate the effect of concomitant CYP3A4 inhibitor with ELAHERE on mirvetuximab soravtansine, free DM4 or s-methyl DM4 exposure. There were 22, 64, and 15 patients taking weak, moderate, or strong Cytochrome P450 3A4 (CYP3A4) inhibitors, respectively, at any time during the study. However, based on the start and end times, ciprofloxacin (a moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor; n = 5) and ranitidine (a weak CYP3A4 inhibitor; n = 6) were the only inhibitors that were administered to multiple patients at or near the start of C1D1 dosing of mirvetuximab soravtansine at 6mg/kg AIBW.
L511: The exposures parameters of mirvetuximab soravtansine, free DM4 or s-methyl DM4 were compared for all of these patients (5 on ciprofloxacin and 6 on ranitidine) were compared with all other patients (n = 231), which showed overlapping exposures (Table 13).
L512: **Table 13: Comparison of Exposures to DM4, S-Methyl-DM4, and mirvetuximab soravtansine in Individual Patients Who Received Concomitant Ciprofloxacin or Ranitidine with Data from Other Patients in Study-0403**

L517: Given the lack of sufficient clinical data with concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitor, the use of strong CYP3A4 inhibitor should be avoided with mirvetuximab soravtansine. In addition, due to the expected increase in unconjugated DM4 and s-methyl DM4 exposure with concomitant use of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, patients receiving concomitant strong CYP3A inhibitors should be closely monitored for signs of toxicity.

L530: ## 7.2. Review Strategy
L531: ## Data Sources:
L532: The clinical and statistical review of mirvetuximab soravtansine is primarily based on the protocol, protocol amendments, clinical study report, patient narratives, Study Data Tabulation Model (SDTM) and Analysis Data Model (ADaM datasets, and case report forms from Study 0417 (SORAYA) submitted by the Applicant on March 28, 2022. The review also includes information for the safety update, submitted on July 25, 2022.
L533: Supportive evidence of efficacy comes from Study 0416 (MIRASOL), an ongoing, phase 3, randomized trial. FDA received an update on ORR/DOR from Study 0416 from a third party during the review cycle for this BLA. The Applicant was blinded to these data. Further details are included in Section 8.1.3 below. FDA received and reviewed topline data only. No datasets were included.
L534: Supportive evidence of safety comes from Study 0403 (FORWARD 1) and Study 0401. FDA reviewed the protocol, clinical study report, patient narratives, datasets, and case report forms from these trials as part of the integrated assessment of safety. Further details are included in Section 8.2 below.
L535: ## Data Quality and Integrity:
L536: Data submitted with this application were in SDTM and ADaM formats. For the most part, data were of adequate quality with adequate coding of preferred terms to allow review. Data were generally consistent with information found in the CRFs. During the review, it was noted that there were issues with the following items in the ocular toxicity data for the safety population: Intraocular pressure measurements were missing, visual acuity measurements were entered in error. For further details, see Ophthalmology General Consult Review by Dr. Wiley Chambers dated 7/8/22. FDA sent the Applicant requests for additional information or clarification of existing information using USUBJID, and for the most part, these were addressed in a timely fashion. In addition, FDA conducted clinical site inspections which are described in Section 4 of this review. There were no significant regulatory violations identified during the inspections and it was concluded that Study IMGN853-0417 was overall properly conducted.
L537: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical and Evaluation
L538: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L539: ## 8.1.1. Study 0417 (SORAYA)
L540: ## Trial Design
L541: Study 0417 was a phase 3, single arm trial evaluating the safety and efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with platinum-resistant high-grade serous epithelial ovarian cancer, primary peritoneal cancer, or fallopian tube cancer with tumors that expressed a high level of FR\alpha. Patients were appropriate for single-agent therapy as the next line of therapy in the opinion of the investigator.
L542: The trial schema is shown in Figure 7.
L543: Figure 7: Study 0417 Trial Design
L544: Source: Applicant AOM slides
L545: ## Study Endpoints
L546: ## Primary endpoint
L547: - ORR as assessed by investigator(INV) according to RECIST v1.1
L548: ## Key secondary endpoint
L549: - DOR as assessed by investigator(INV)
L550: ## Additional secondary endpoints
L551: - TEAEs, laboratory test results, physical exam findings, vital signs
- CA-125 response determined using GCIG criteria defined in Appendix C
- PFS as assessed by investigator according to RECIST v1.1
- OS
- ORR, DOR, and PFS as assessed by blinded independent central review according to RECIST v1.1
L552: ## Key Eligibility Criteria
L553: ## Inclusion criteria
L554: - Female patients \ge 18 years of age
- Diagnosis of high-grade serous epithelial ovarian cancer, primary peritoneal cancer, or fallopian tube cancer
- Platinum-resistant disease defined as:If patients have received only 1 line of platinum-based therapy, they must have received at least 4 cycles of a platinum agent, must have experienced a CR/PR, and must have progressed &gt;3 months but \le 6 months after the last dose
- Patients who have received 2-3 lines of platinum-based therapy must have progressed on or within 6 months after the date of the last dose of platinum. Patients with platinum-refractory disease during front-line treatment are excluded.
L555: **Reviewer Comment:** Definitions for “platinum-resistant” and “platinum-refractory” disease vary. However, we note that in some definitions, patients who experience PD after completing 1L therapy but &lt;3 months after the last dose may be considered to have platinum-resistant disease. Those patients were excluded from enrolling to Study 0417.
L556: We also note that patients who experience PD while receiving 2L+ platinum-based therapy may be considered to have platinum-refractory disease in some definitions. These patients were permitted to enroll to Study 0417.
L557: - Radiographic progression on or after most recent line of therapy
- Willing to provide archival tumor tissue or undergo a fresh biopsy with a low-risk, medically routine procedure for immunohistochemistry (IHC) to confirm FR\alpha positivity
- Tumor must be positive for FR\alpha expression as defined by the Ventana FOLR1 assay
- At least 1 lesion that meets the definition of measurable disease by RECIST v1.1 (as determined by the investigator)
L558: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L559: - Received at least 1 but no more than 3 prior systemic lines of therapy, including at least 1 line of therapy containing bevacizumab, and must be appropriate for single agent therapy as next line of treatment. Notes:(Neo)adjuvant treatment is considered 1 line of treatment
- Maintenance therapy is considered part of the preceding line of treatment (not a separate line of treatment)
- If patients switch therapies due to toxicity without progression of disease (PD), both therapies will be considered as the same line of treatment
- Hormonal therapy will count as a separate line of therapy unless it was given as maintenance treatment
- Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) Performance Status (PS) of 0 or 1
- Completed prior therapies in the following timeframes:Systemic anticancer therapy within 5 half-lives or 4 weeks (whichever is shorter) prior to first dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine
- Focal radiation at least 2 weeks prior to first dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine
- Completed any major surgery at least 4 weeks prior to first dose
- Recovered all prior treatment-related toxicities to Grade \le 1 (except alopecia)
- Adequate organ function defined as:Absolute neutrophil count (ANC) \ge 1.5 \times 10^9/L without granulocyte colony stimulating factor (G-CSF) in prior 10 days, hemoglobin \ge 9.9 g/dL without packed RBC transfusion in prior 21 days, platelet count \ge 100 \times 10^9/L without platelet transfusion in past 10 days.
- Serum creatinine \le 1.5 \times the upper limit of normal (ULN), aspartate aminotransferase (AST) and alanine aminotransferase (ALT) \le 3 \times ULN, serum total bilirubin \le 1.5 \times ULN (or \le 3 \times ULN for patients with diagnosis of Gilbert syndrome), serum albumin \ge 2 g/dL
L560: - Women of child-bearing potential must have a negative pregnancy test within 4 days prior to first dose and must agree to use highly effective contraceptive methods while receiving mirvetuximab soravtansine and for at least 3 months after the last dose
L561: ## Exclusion Criteria
L562: - Male patients
- Patients with tumors with endometrioid, clear cell, mucinous, or sarcomatous histology, or mixed tumors containing any of these histologies, or low-grade/borderline ovarian tumor
- Primary platinum-refractory disease defined as disease that did not respond to (CR or PR) or progressed within 3 months of the last dose of first-line platinum-containing chemotherapy
- Prior wide-field radiotherapy affecting at least 20% of bone marrow
- Patients with &gt; Grade 1 peripheral neuropathy per Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) criteria
- Patients with active or chronic corneal disorders, history of corneal transplantation or active ocular conditions requiring ongoing treatment/monitoring (e.g., uncontrolled glaucoma, wet age-related macular degeneration requiring intravitreal injections, active diabetic retinopathy with macular edema, macular degeneration, papilledema, and/or monocular vision)
- Patients with concurrent serious illness or clinically relevant active infection, including but not limited to the following: active hepatitis B or hepatitis C infection, human immunodeficiency virus (HIV), active cytomegalovirus (CMV), or any other concurrent infection requiring IV antibiotics within 2 weeks prior to the first dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine
- History of multiple sclerosis or other demyelinating disease or Lambert-Eaton syndrome
- Clinically significant cardiac disease including, but not limited to any of the following: myocardial infarction ≤ 6 months prior to first dose, unstable angina pectoris, uncontrolled CHF (New York Heart Association (NYHA) &gt; class II), uncontrolled ≥ Grade 3 hypertension, uncontrolled cardiac arrhythmias
L563: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L564: - History of a hemorrhagic or ischemic stroke within 6 months
- History of cirrhotic liver disease (Child-Pugh Class B or C)
- History of noninfectious interstitial lung disease including noninfectious pneumonitis
- Currently requiring use of folate-containing supplements (i.e., folate deficiency)
- Prior mirvetuximab soravtansine or another FR\alpha-targeting agent
- Prior hypersensitivity to monoclonal antibodies
- Untreated or symptomatic central nervous system (CNS) metastases
- Women who are pregnant or breastfeeding
- History of another malignancy within 3 years (patients with tumors with negligible risk of metastasis or death may enroll)
- Prior known hypersensitivity to the investigational product or excipients
L565: ## In Vitro Diagnostic
L566: Assessment of tumor tissue for FR\alpha expression occurred using the Ventana FOLR1 assay. This was an immunohistochemistry (IHC) assay which detects FR\alpha in formalin-fixed, paraffin embedded (FFPE) tissue specimens using the Ventana OptiView DAB IHC Detection Kit. Testing with the assay will occur at a central laboratory. The scoring method for the assay was based on PS2+ which refers to membrane staining intensity of 2 or greater. The pathologist assessed the percentage of tumors cells with moderate (2) or strong (3) membrane staining. FR\alpha positivity was defined as \ge 75\% of cells with level 2 or level 3 membrane staining intensity. Refer to the CDRH consult for more details regarding the in vitro diagnostic device.
L567: ## Treatment Regimen
L568: Patients received mirvetuximab soravtansine 6 mg/kg of adjusted ideal body weight (AIBW) administered on Day 1 of every 3-week cycle. Patients continued treatment until PD, unacceptable toxicity, withdrawal of consent, death, or if the trial was terminated.
L569: All patients received premedication with 325-650 mg PO acetaminophen, 10 mg IV dexamethasone, and 25-50 mg PO/IV diphenhydramine approximately 30 minutes prior to each
L570: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L571: infusion. An anti-emetic medication was recommended before each dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine.
L572: For repeat cycles, patients must have had an ANC \ge 1.5 \times 10^9/L, platelet count \ge 100 \times 10^9/L, ocular disorders must have been recovered to \le Grade 1 or baseline, pneumonitis must have been recovered to \le Grade 1, and all other non-hematologic toxicities must have been \le Grade 2 or baseline.
L573: Patients could not take folate supplements during the trial.
L574: ## Ocular Plan
L575: All patients were required to use corticosteroid eye drops as prescribed by the treating physician unless the risk outweighed the benefit in the opinion of the ophthalmologist or treatment physician. Patients self-administered 1% prednisolone drops 6 times daily on Days -1 to 4, and 4 times daily on Days 5 to 8 of each cycle. Alternate corticosteroid eye drops could have been prescribed.
L576: All patients were required to use lubricating artificial tears on a daily basis. Patients were told to wait at least 15 minutes after using corticosteroid eye drops to use artificial tears.
L577: Patients were advised to avoid contact lenses. Patients were told to use baby shampoo and a soft washcloth to clean eyes and could have used warm compresses at bedtime to decrease ocular inflammation. Patients were told to wear UVA/UVB sunglasses during the trial. Also refer to dose modifications and study assessments.
L578: ## Tumor Assessments
L579: Tumor imaging assessments with computerized tomography (CT) or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) of chest, abdomen, and pelvis occurred every 6 weeks (\pm 1 week) from cycle 1, day 1 (C1D1) for the first 36 weeks, and then every 12 weeks (\pm 3 weeks) thereafter until progression of disease, death, new anticancer treatment, or withdrawal of consent. Patients who discontinued treatment for reasons other than PD continued to receive tumor imaging assessments until PD or start of new anticancer therapy. All patients who discontinued the study drug were followed every 3 months (\pm 1 month) for survival follow-up until death, lost to follow-up, withdrawal of consent, or the end of the trial, whichever occurred first.
L580: CA-125 assessments were also planned to occur at same intervals as imaging assessments.
L581: ## Safety Assessments
L582: **Safety assessments included the following:**
L583: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L584: - Vital signs, weight, physical exam, and ECOG PS assessment at baseline, Day 1 of every cycle, end-of-treatment (EOT), and 30-day follow-up
- Pregnancy test at baseline, Day 1 of every cycle, and 30-day follow-up
- Hematology and chemistry labs at baseline, Day 1 of every cycle, EOT, and 30-day follow-up
- 12-lead EKG at baseline, and C1D1
- Ophthalmic exams at baseline, EOT, and 30-day follow-up. If an ocular TEAE occurred, exam would occur every other cycle.
- Ocular symptom assessment at baseline, Day 1 of every cycle, EOT, and 30-day follow-up
- Survival phone screen every 3 months following EOT

L617: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L618: The primary endpoint was investigator-assessed confirmed ORR which included a best response of either CR or PR. Along with a point estimate, the 95% exact confidence interval (CI) was estimated by the Clopper-Pearson method. ORR was based on the Efficacy Evaluable population, which included all patients who had measurable lesions at baseline and received at least one dose of mirvetuximab.
L619: The trial was planned to enroll approximately 105 patients, yielding 90% power to detect a difference in ORR of 12% (24% vs. 12%) using a 1-sided binomial test. The target significance level was 0.025 (1-sided). The actual significance level of the test was 0.0242. These targets assumed an ORR of 12% under the null hypothesis and 24% under the alternate hypothesis.
L620: Initial DOR assessment was planned to occur when ORR was determined. A second DOR analysis was also planned to occur 12 months after primary analysis of ORR.
L621: **Reviewer comment:** FDA does not conduct formal testing of ORR endpoints. FDA assesses the ORR point estimate and 95% CI in the context of other factors including DOR, safety and tolerability, and other available therapies.
L622: ## Protocol Amendments
L623: The key changes in each protocol amendment are listed below:
L624: ## Protocol Amendment 1 (December 18, 2019)
L625: - Increased the number of enrolled patients from 100 to 110 patients, to have 105 evaluable patients rather than 91 evaluable patients.
- Added that patients who discontinued mirvetuximab soravtansine for reasons other than PD prior to week 36 and who did not initiate other anticancer therapy would continue receiving scans every 6 weeks until week 36 (rather than every 12 weeks until week 36). Patients would continue to receive scans every 12 months thereafter.
- Revised the definition of the efficacy evaluable population as those who received at least one dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine and have measurable lesions at baseline per RECIST v1.1.
- Added an exploratory objective to evaluate tumor-based biomarkers predictive of response to mirvetuximab soravtansine
L626: ## Protocol Amendment 1.1 (June 18, 2020)
L627: - Added a reference to the Investigator statement to ensure that the investigator had read the Ventana Assay Protocol Plan
L628: ## Protocol Amendment 1.2 (July 22, 2020)
L629: - Added recommendation to perform monthly pregnancy tests in women of childbearing potential for 3 months following last dose of IP
- Revised list of contraceptive methods to include those considered highly effective according to the Clinical Trials Facilitation and Coordination Group (CTFG) guidelines
L630: ## Protocol Amendment 2 (August 28, 2020)
L631: - Updated inclusion criterion regarding prior lines of therapy to state that patients who had only received 1 prior line of therapy must have received at least 4 cycles and progressed in &gt;3 months but ≤ 6 months. Patients who had received 2-3 prior lines could have progressed on or within 6 months following last dose of platinum. (Prior to this update, all patients, regardless of prior lines of therapy, must have received at least 4 cycles of treatment.)
- Added an explicit statement that patients who had disease that was platinum-refractory during 1L treatment were excluded.
- Stated that if ocular symptoms last &gt;28 patients, patients may resume mirvetuximab soravtansine with or without dose reduction if they experienced clinical benefit and if agreed upon by Sponsor and Investigator.
L632: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L633: - Modified dose modification criteria to add that recurrent G2 ocular toxicity will require 1 level dose reduction and recurrent G3 ocular toxicity will require 1 level dose reduction.
- Added that mirvetuximab soravtansine will be discontinued for G3 pneumonitis and G4 ocular toxicity.
- Added a definition for woman of childbearing potential.
- Added that end of study will occur 12 months after ORR analysis. Either the trial will be amended to allow patients receiving mirvetuximab soravtansine to continue or patients will be given the option to rollover to a long-term extension trial.
L634: ## Protocol Amendment 2.1 (September 11, 2020)
L635: - Added a reference to the Investigator statement to ensure that the investigator had read the Ventana Assay Protocol Plan.
L636: **Reviewer comment:** *These changes appear to be the same changes described in the summary of changes for Protocol Amendment 1.1 but it does not appear that these changes were implemented until Protocol Amendment 2.1. The changes are relatively minor and not expected to affect interpretation of trial results.*
L637: ## Administrative Letter (May 12, 2021)
L638: - Recommended that the last ophthalmologic exam be performed at the 30 day follow-up visit rather than end-of-treatment
L639: ## 8.1.2. Study Results
L640: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L641: The Applicant conducted Study 0417 in accordance with principles outlined in the International Conference on Harmonization (ICH) Guideline for GCP. Safety monitoring was consistent with the ICH-GCP E6 (R2) guidelines. The Applicant has a GCP audit program to ensure compliance with applicable regulations including ICH E6 R2, and federal, state, and local regulations. The Applicant conducted independent site audits. The Applicant's GCP compliance program determined that Study 0417 was conducted in compliance with GCP and that the trial data are reliable.
L642: ## Financial Disclosure
L643: Financial disclosure information is summarized in Section 19.2. It is unlikely that the disclosable financial interests affected the conduct of Study 0417.
L644: ## Patient Disposition
L645: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L646: There were n=106 patients enrolled to Study 0417. Of these n=106 patients, n=105 patients were included in the efficacy evaluable by INV population by the Applicant. One patient was excluded due to lacking measurable disease at trial baseline per INV. FDA excluded one additional patient from the efficacy evaluable by INV population because this patient had a platinum-free interval of 18 months, i.e. did not have platinum-resistant disease. FDA's efficacy evaluable by INV population included n=104 patients. Of the n=106 patients, n=95 were included in the efficacy evaluable by BICR population by the Applicant. Eleven patients were excluded due to lacking measurable disease at baseline per BICR.

L671: ## **Reviewer comment:**
L672: *The Surveillance, Epidemiology, and End Results (SEER) database provides data regarding the relative incidence of ovarian cancer cases by race. The relative incidence of ovarian cancer per 100,000 women is 13.1 in White patients, 10.2 in American Indian patients, 9.0 in Black patients, and 8.4 in Asian/Pacific Islander patients. Regarding ethnicity, the relative incidence of ovarian cancer cases per 100,000 women is 9.4 for Hispanic patients and 13.0 for patients who are not Hispanic. Although there is a lower incidence of ovarian cancer among Black patients, they may experience worse outcomes. One study showed that Black patients experienced the lowest 5-year survival compared to White patients, Hispanic patients, and Asian patients at each stage of disease (Goodman 2003).*
L673: *It is challenging to determine the distribution by race or ethnicity of patients in the U.S. with advanced ovarian cancer. However, the FDA review team does not consider the Study 0417 trial population to be representative of a U.S. based population with ovarian cancer with respect to race and ethnicity. In terms of race, Study 0417 enrolled 96% patients who were White, 2% of patients who were Asian, and 2% of patients who were "Other" (further details unknown). In terms of ethnicity, Study 0417 enrolled only 2% of patients who were Hispanic or Latino (Park 2017).*
L674: *FDA issued a PMC regarding race and ethnicity. FDA recommended that the Applicant conduct an integrated analysis including data from clinical trials and other data sources to further characterize the safety and efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients from racial and ethnic minority groups. See Section 13 for more details.*

L681: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L682: The Applicant monitored drug accountability to ensure that mirvetuximab soravtansine was only given to qualified personnel. Treatment compliance was monitored and captured in the eCRF including data regarding the time of the dose, the actual dose administered, dose modifications, and other details regarding mirvetuximab soravtansine administration. Of note, ocular toxicity and infusion-related reactions (IRRs) are safety signals of concern in the mirvetuximab soravtansine development program. Details regarding concomitant medication administration for prophylaxis and management of these toxicities can be found in 8.2: Review of Safety.
L683: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L684: The primary efficacy results for INV-assessed ORR are shown in Table 26. Mirvetuximab provided an ORR of 31.7% ([95% CI: 22.9, 41.6] by investigator) in participants with platinum resistant, advanced, high-grade epithelial ovarian, primary peritoneal, or fallopian tube cancers with high folate receptor-alpha expression.
L685: The median DOR in the original BLA submission, based upon a data cutoff (DCO) date of 11/16/21, was 5.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 7.7 months) with a range of 1.4+ to 9.8 months.
L686: As part of the 120-Day Update, the Applicant included information regarding DOR. The DCO date for the DOR update was 4/29/22 and included an additional 5 months of follow-up. With longer follow-up, among the 33 patients in the INV Efficacy Evaluable population with a
L687: confirmed CR or PR, the updated median DOR per RECIST was 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7). These results are shown in Table 26.

L698: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L699: ## ORR and DOR by BICR
L700: ORR by BICR was 29.4% (95% CI: 20.8, 39.3). A CR was achieved by 4.9% of participants, and a PR was achieved by 24.5% of participants. The median DOR was 11.7 months (95% CI: 5.0, NR) and ranged from 1.3+ to 11.7 months (data cutoff: 11/16/21). At the 120-Day Update, median
L701: DOR by BICR per RECIST 1.1 was not reached (95% CI: 5.0 months to not reached) and ranged from 2.5 to 12.7+ months (data cutoff: 4/29/22).

L718: ## Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L719: ## 8.1.3. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L720: The confirmatory, randomized trial, Study 0416 (MIRASOL, NCT04209855), was ongoing during FDA's review of the current application. During a Type B pre-BLA meeting held in January 2022 to discuss the topline results from Study 0417, FDA indicated that they would request ORR and DOR data from the ongoing trial Study 0416 during review of the Study 0417 results, in order to achieve an early indication as to the outcome of trial that would be used to confirm clinical benefit, in the event of an accelerated approval based on Study 0417. During the current review, FDA sent a formal request to the Applicant, requesting blinded ORR and DOR data from both arms of the ongoing, randomized trial, Study 0416. To maintain trial integrity, the Applicant used a third-party vendor to generate the requested results and put in place procedures to prevent anyone from the company from accessing these results, so that the Applicant would remain blinded.
L721: Study 0416 is an ongoing, randomized, phase 3 clinical comparing mirvetuximab soravtansine to investigator's choice chemotherapy in patients with platinum-resistant, high-grade epithelial ovarian, primary peritoneal, or fallopian tube cancer, whose tumors have a high level of FR\alpha expression. Patients should be appropriate for single agent chemotherapy to enroll to this trial, can have received 1-3 prior lines of systemic therapy, and are permitted (but not required) to have received bevacizumab. Patients are also permitted to have received prior PARP inhibitors. Study 0416 is occurring in a similar treatment to Study 0417, except patients were required to have received prior bevacizumab to enroll to SORAYA. Options for investigator's choice chemotherapy included paclitaxel, PLD or topotecan. The primary endpoint is PFS and secondary endpoints include ORR, DOR, and OS.
L722: The data cut-off date for the ORR and DOR data from Study 0416 was May 13, 2022. FDA only reviewed topline results and did not receive datasets. The topline ORR and DOR results from Study 0416 were supportive of the efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who have received 1-3 prior lines of systemic therapy.
L723: **Reviewer Comment:** At the time of the ORR/DOR update, Study 0416 had enrolled 361 (84%) patients out of the planned 430 patients.
L724: ## 8.1.4. **Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness**
L725: FDA's conclusions regarding efficacy are primarily based on results from Study 0417(SORAYA), a phase 3, single arm trial evaluating the safety and efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant, high-grade serous epithelial ovarian cancer, primary peritoneal cancer, or fallopian tube cancer. In Study 0417, the ORR assessed by INV per RECIST v1.1 was 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6). Median DOR was 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7). Results for ORR and DOR as assessed by BICR were generally consistent with results as assessed by INV, with an overall concordance of 86% for ORR assessment between INV and BICR. ORR results were also generally consistent across subgroups.
L726: Patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer, particularly those who have received prior bevacizumab, represent an area of unmet medical need. Treatment options are limited and generally include single agent chemotherapy, with ORRs ranging from 6-27% in previous trials (**Table 2**). DOR data are scant. An important caveat when interpreting these response rates is that many of the trials with single agent chemotherapies were conducted before establishment of a 1L treatment regimen containing a platinum-based doublet \pm bevacizumab followed by maintenance treatment with bevacizumab, niraparib, olaparib, or olaparib + bevacizumab as standard of care for advanced ovarian cancer. The efficacy of the single agent chemotherapies for treatment of platinum-resistant ovarian cancer may be less than seen in historical trials.
L727: A better benchmark for available therapies in the current treatment era may be the chemotherapy control arm from recent trials in platinum-resistant ovarian cancer. These trials are shown in Table 2, and the ORR is 12-13% and median DOR is 4-7.4 months. In addition, not all patients included in these trials had received prior bevacizumab, and many patients had received only 1-2 prior lines of treatment. All patients enrolled to Study 0417 had received prior bevacizumab, and 50% of patients had received 3 prior lines of treatment, representing a more heavily pre-treated population. There are no available therapies specifically for patients with FR\alpha-positive platinum-resistant ovarian cancer.
L728: To support the efficacy results from Study 0417, FDA requested blinded topline ORR/DOR data from Study 0416, an ongoing randomized trial in a similar treatment setting to Study 0417. These results were supportive of the efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with FR\alpha- positive platinum-resistant ovarian cancer.
L729: Given the results from Study 0417, supported by additional data from Study 0416, and considering the current therapeutic landscape for patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer who have received 1-3 prior lines of therapy, including prior bevacizumab, FDA considers an ORR of 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6) and a median DOR of 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7) to represent an improvement over available therapies. The review team determines that these results are reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit for mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant ovarian cancer who have received 1-3 prior systemic treatment regimens. Patients will be selected for treatment using VENTANA FOLR1 (FOLR1-2.1) RxDx Assay. Confirmation of clinical benefit will be based on results from Study 0416, the
L730: ongoing randomized clinical trial comparing mirvetuximab soravtansine to investigator's choice chemotherapy in patients with FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant ovarian cancer.
L731: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L732: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L733: ## Data:
L734: The safety profile summarized in this application consists of data from 3 clinical trials of mirvetuximab soravtansine including 464 patients. All patients received the recommended starting dose of 6mg/kg based on AIBW of mirvetuximab soravtansine. The number of patients in each of the safety analysis groups is described below.
L735: The Study 0417 group includes 106 patients in the safety population, and this was the pivotal trial submitted for this application. All patients had high-grade serous ovarian cancer. All patients had received 1-3 prior lines of systemic treatment and 100% had received bevacizumab. Tumor samples were assessed to have high FR\alpha expression in all patients.
L736: The pooled safety set included all epithelial ovarian cancer (EOC) patients who received mirvetuximab soravtansine 6mg/kg AIBW Q3W on one of 3 studies, including Studies 0401, 0403, 0417. This included EOC patients (expansion cohorts 1, 3, and 5) in Study 0401 (N=113), the Phase 3 portion of Study 0403 (N=245), and all patients in Study 0417 (N=106). Of note, 142 of the total n=464 patients had tumors with FR\alpha high expression according to the criteria used in Study 0417.
L737: The primary evidence of safety is derived from the Study 0417 data, as the subjects represent a more homogeneous patient population than the pooled data. Patients enrolled in Study 0417 had tumors with high FR\alpha expression and received the same protocol-specified mitigation measures and dose modification. Key features of Study 0417 compared to the pooled analysis data sets from Study 0401, 0403 include:
L738: - Study 0417 had implementation of the most up to date recommended dose modifications and mitigation strategies for AEs. In particular, this included changes in the ocular toxicity mitigation strategies from prophylactic corticosteroid eye drops for 10 days (Study 0401) to 8 days per cycle (Studies 0403, 0417).
- Study 0417 had a more homogeneous population based upon FR\alpha High-expression
- The eligibility criteria for Study 0417 included limitations on the number of allowable prior therapies (1 to 3 prior systemic lines of anticancer therapy, including mandatory receipt of prior bevacizumab).
L739: **Reviewer comment:** There were no obvious discrepancies identified between the analysis datasets and the information provided in the Clinical Study Reports. The applicant's categorization of data and coding methods were appropriate. The preferred terms (PTs) listed in
L740: *the datasets adequately represented the investigator-recorded term. FDA has noted some minor differences in grouped terms (GTs) noted in sections below. FDA has used their analyses for the data tables in Section 8.2 unless otherwise indicated. The key focus for the safety review was on data majority of the data on was obtained from Study 0417. However, pooled analyses using data from ovarian cancer patients on studies 0401 and 0403 were also reviewed for TEAEs and AEs of Special Interest including peripheral neuropathy, ocular toxicities, and pneumonitis to confirm that safety for the pooled population did not reveal additional safety signals.*
L741: *The size of the safety population is different from the size of the efficacy population for the single arm trial 0417. The safety population on Study 0417 consisted of all patients who received at least one dose of Mirvetuximab (n=106). The efficacy population included all patients who had efficacy evaluable disease, as per INV assessment, and this led to exclusion of one patient due to lack of measurable disease and one patient due to protocol violation. Therefore, the efficacy population included n=104 patients.*

The pooled cohort included 464 patients from studies 0401, 0403, and study 0417. The analyses focused on assessment of common TEAEs and AEs of Special Interest including peripheral neuropathy, ocular toxicities, and pneumonitis.
