L756: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L757: The safety analysis for this BLA is primarily based on data from 106 safety-evaluable patients who received at least 1 dose of study drug and who had at least one post-baseline safety assessment Study 0417. But as noted, the Applicant also provided datasets for the two other clinical studies, the randomized Study 0403 and a dose-escalation phase of the FIH study, Study 0401. The pooled safety cohort including 464 patients from studies 0401, 0403, and study 0417 is considered adequate for an assessment of the safety of mirvetuximab soravtansine.
L758: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L759: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L760: FDA review of the ocular toxicity data submitted by the Applicant revealed that there were visual acuity values that appeared to have been entered into the database in error for study 0403. In addition the BCVA and IOP shift tables in the CSR for Study 0417 had missing data. The Applicant resolved these issues through response to information request on July 18, 2022.
L761: **Reviewer's Comments:** Visual acuity and intraocular pressure data were originally not considered to be interpretable because of a large number of missing values; additionally there were limited assessments by fundoscopy, dilated slit lamp and evaluations of tear production. This was noted via ophthalmology consultation with Dr. Wiley Chambers and is described in his consult review. The subsequent response and submission of additional information by the Applicant was also reviewed by Dr. Chambers and was considered acceptable to proceed with safety assessment. Though not all of the missing data was obtained, the response was considered adequate to proceed with safety review and did not change the overall safety assessment for the application.
L762: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L763: Safety and tolerability assessment was based on the frequency of deaths, AEs, serious adverse events (SAEs), AEs leading to discontinuation, AEs leading to interruptions, select AEs, clinical laboratory assessments (hematology, serum chemistry, liver, kidney and thyroid function tests), and vital sign measurements. AEs were coded using the MedDRA version 20.0. AEs and laboratory values were graded for severity using the NCI CTCAE version 4.0. Adverse events were categorized by System Organ Class (SOC) and Preferred Term (PT). The Applicant pre-specified adverse events of special interest (AESIs) based on Event of Clinical Interest Guidance. For this application, AESIs comprised of pneumonitis, ocular toxicities, peripheral neuropathy, and infusion-related reactions.
L764: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L765: The following assessments were planned starting on Cycle 1 Day 1 and continued at regular intervals as pre-specified in the protocol:
L766: - Vital signs including temperature, blood pressure, heart rate, and respiratory rate
L767: were measured before start of each infusion.
L768: - AEs were monitored continuously throughout the study from the time of informed consent until 30 days after the patient's last study treatment.
- A complete physical examination, including assessments of general appearance, skin, head (eyes, ears, nose, and throat), neck, lungs, heart, abdomen, back, lymph nodes, extremities, and neurological system, were completed at Screening and at the 30-Day Follow-up Visit. At all other study visits while on mirvetuximab, patients had symptom-directed PEs.
- Complete blood count (CBCs) with differential, including white blood count (WBC), with 5 part differential, hemoglobin, hematocrit, and platelet count Serum chemistry tests (blood urea nitrogen, calcium, carbon dioxide, chloride, glucose, serum creatinine, sodium, potassium, calcium, magnesium, phosphorus), Liver function tests including AST, ALT, total bilirubin, alkaline phosphatase, albumin (could be performed up to 4 days prior to D1 of each cycle, and as clinically indicated while on treatment, with results reviewed before each mirvetuximab administration)PT or INR/aPTT performed at screening
- Urinalysis performed at screening
- Serum CA-125 assessments were performed within 14 days and prior to the first dose of study drug, prior to dosing on Day 1 of every subsequent cycle
- Radiographic tumor evaluation by CT or MRI of chest, abdomen, and pelvis were performed within 28 days before first dose of study drug and every 6 weeks (\pm 1 week) from C1D1 for the first 36 weeks on study and every 12 weeks (\pm 3 week) thereafter
- Pregnancy screening for women of childbearing potential were completed via a serum beta-human chorionic gonadotropin (\beta-hCG) or urine pregnancy test not more than 14 days before the first dose of study drug and urine or serum pregnancy tests within 4 days of each study drug administration and at the 30-Day Follow-up visit. After the 30-day follow-up visit it was recommended to perform monthly pregnancy tests for 3 months after the last dose of MIRV. Additional testing could be performed in accordance with institutional requirements or local regulation.
- BRCA Mutation status was recorded from prior testing (information was in the source record).
- A standard, single 12-lead ECG will be performed within 28 days prior to first dose
L769: **Ocular symptoms assessment:** Performed before the start of each cycle by the treating physician or other qualified individual. For patients reporting &gt; CTCAE Grade 1 ocular symptoms, study drug will be held until the patient is evaluated by an ophthalmologist for a complete examination. The ocular symptom assessment was also performed at EOT and the 30-Day Follow-up visit. An ophthalmic examination was performed at Screening (within 14 days prior to first dose of study drug) by an ophthalmologist and included the following: distant visual acuity, best corrected visual acuity, slit lamp
L770: examination, intraocular pressure measurement, and indirect fundoscopy. Patients who experienced ocular TEAEs while on study had a complete ophthalmologic exam performed at the emergence of the symptoms and at every other cycle thereafter. All patients who had an ophthalmic exam on study treatment (post-baseline) had a complete ophthalmologic exam performed at the EOT visit or 30-Day Follow-up visit
L771: Patients were assessed for toxicity prior to each dose. All SAEs were collected up until 30 days after the last dose of trial treatment. AEs were documented in the clinical trial database and monitored continuously throughout the study from the time of informed consent until 30 days after the patient's last study treatment. SAEs were followed by ImmunoGen Pharmacovigilance until resolution, stabilization or return to baseline. Beyond this defined reporting period, any unsolicited SAE assessed as related to the study drug by the Investigator and reported to ImmunoGen were collected and processed.
L772: **Reviewer comment:** Routine clinical testing of patients enrolled in Study 0417 was generally considered appropriate for evaluating AEs related to Mirvetuximab. However, FDA did not find the assessments of ocular toxicity based on symptoms alone to be adequate for ensuring the proper monitoring. Routine ophthalmologic examination for monitoring was recommended for future studies of Mirvetuximab and in the USPI and further details regarding ocular toxicities are included below.

L779: ## Deaths
L780: There were 33 (31%) deaths in the overall safety population of 106 patients that received mirvetuximab. The majority (25%) of deaths were due to disease progression. Three patients in Study 0417 experienced AEs that led to death. The AEs leading to death were intestinal obstruction (N=2), and respiratory failure (N=1).
L781: A summary of deaths within 30 days of last study treatment is provided in Table 32. In Study 0417, 7 patients died within 30 days of study drug administration. Four of these deaths were due to disease progression and 2 of these were attributed to adverse events, 1 patient experienced pneumonitis and died of respiratory failure and one patient died of a small bowel obstruction. There was 1 patient who developed a colonic obstruction and was hospitalized and subsequently died, but no imaging was conducted to confirm the cause.
L782: Narrative summaries for each TEAE leading to death are provided in the respective CSRs.

L785: **Reviewer Comments:** FDA conducted an analysis of all deaths occurring on Study 0417. Of the 33 deaths occurring on Study 0417, the majority were due to progression of disease. Seven patients died within 30 days of study drug. The Applicant attributed 2 deaths to adverse events. One was a patient who was noted to die of respiratory failure which was in the setting of pneumonitis. Another death occurred in a patient who experienced a small bowel obstruction and died, but this event was assessed to constitute an adverse event. FDA reviewed the narratives and case report forms for these 2 patients and agreed with the Applicant's assessment, that these 2 deaths were due to adverse events. There was one additional death, which the Applicant attributed to an "other" cause, but according to the narrative, the patient developed colonic obstruction during cycle 2 of therapy, was hospitalized and died after
L786: *receiving palliative sedation. This was not counted as a death due to adverse event by FDA. Additional information for these 3 deaths is described below.*
L787: ## **PATIENT ID# [REDACTED](b) (6) -Respiratory Failure likely secondary to pneumonitis**
L788: The patient was an 85-year-old white female who received the first infusion of mirvetuximab soravtansine on [REDACTED](b) (6). On [REDACTED](b) (6) 21 (Day 43), a CT scan of the abdomen (including pelvis) showed small bilateral effusions with malignant looking pleural thickening and patchy ground-glass changes noted posteriorly in the lungs and an adverse event (AE) of Grade 1 Pneumonitis was reported. On [REDACTED](b) (6) (Day 54), the subject was admitted for a serious adverse event (SAE) of Grade 3 Esophagitis. The last infusion of mirvetuximab soravtansine prior to onset of this event was the Cycle 3, Day 1 infusion administered on [REDACTED](b) (6) (Day 43).
L789: On [REDACTED](b) (6) (Day 62), the subject experienced increased tiredness and shortness of breath with basal crackles and was subsequently hospitalized for an SAE that also met criteria for an adverse event of interest (AEI) of Grade 3 Respiratory failure. The last infusion of mirvetuximab soravtansine prior to the onset of this event was the Cycle 3, Day 1 infusion administered on [REDACTED](b) (6) (Day 43) and mirvetuximab soravtansine was permanently withdrawn due to this SAE/AEI. On [REDACTED](b) (6) (Day 69), the SAE/AEI of Grade 3 Respiratory failure worsened to Grade 4 Respiratory failure. The following day, the subject returned to oncology/palliative care. A chest x-ray compared to the chest x-ray on [REDACTED](b) (6) (Day 68) revealed persistent diffuse groundglass opacities throughout the mid and lower zones of both lungs, no definite change compared to recent radiographs, compatible with interstitial pneumonia. On [REDACTED](b) (6) (Day 71), 28 days after the last infusion of mirvetuximab soravtansine, the patient died due to respiratory failure. The SAE/AEI of Grade 5 Respiratory failure was assessed by the investigator as possibly related to mirvetuximab soravtansine. The autopsy reported the cause of death as advanced metastatic carcinoma of the ovary complicated by diffuse alveolar damage on a background of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis with recent clinical bronchopneumonia.
L790: **Reviewer comment:** FDA acknowledged that the patient had advanced ovarian cancer, but considered the cause of death to be most likely due to respiratory failure in the setting of pneumonitis caused by mirvetuximab. The Applicant agreed that because pneumonitis was a plausible contributor to the patient's respiratory failure and death, this patient is described in product labeling as having had an adverse event (pneumonitis) leading to death.
L791: ## **PATIENT ID# [REDACTED](b) (6) -Small Bowel Obstruction, AE leading to Death**
L792: This patient received the first and only infusion of mirvetuximab soravtansine 6 mg/kg AIBW on [REDACTED](b) (6). An x-ray showed small bowel dilation and the subject was hospitalized due to a serious adverse event (SAE) of Grade 3 Small intestinal obstruction. On [REDACTED](b) (6) (Day 18), 17 days after the last infusion of mirvetuximab soravtansine, the event worsened and the subject died due to an SAE of Grade 5 Small intestinal obstruction. A death certificate was not completed, and an autopsy was not performed. The SAEs of Grade 3 Small
L793: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L794: intestinal obstruction and Grade 5 Small intestinal obstruction were assessed by the investigator as not related to mirvetuximab soravtansine. The events were noted to be due to disease progression.
L795: **Reviewer comment:** FDA agrees with the investigator's assessment of attribution of death to disease progression. However, this event of small intestinal obstruction was included in product labeling by the Applicant noted as an AE leading to death, mainly given the timing of the occurrence, within 30 days of the last dose of mirvetuximab.
L796: ## PATIENT ID # [REDACTED] (b) (6) - Palliative Sedation
L797: The subject was a 69-year-old white female who was diagnosed with Stage IV epithelial ovarian cancer in [REDACTED] (b) (6). Prior therapies for treatment of epithelial ovarian cancer included carboplatin and paclitaxel ([REDACTED] (b) (6) 6 cycles), bevacizumab ([REDACTED] (b) (6) 6 cycles), pegylated liposomal doxorubicin hydrochloride and trabectedin ([REDACTED] (b) (6) 7 cycles), and carboplatin and gemcitabine ([REDACTED] (b) (6) 12 cycles). The subject received the first infusion of mirvetuximab soravtansine at 6 mg/kg AIBW on [REDACTED] (b) (6). On [REDACTED] (b) (6) (Day 24), the subject presented to an outside hospital with symptoms of worsening colonic obstruction and was hospitalized for a serious adverse event (SAE) of Grade 3 Large intestinal obstruction. On [REDACTED] (b) (6) (Day 31), 9 days after the last infusion of mirvetuximab soravtansine, the subject's abdominal pain worsened, and palliative sedation was initiated. The patient was noted to have died due to the palliative sedation. No autopsy was performed, and no further information was provided. This same day, the SAE of Grade 3 Large intestinal obstruction was considered resolved with sequelae and was assessed by the investigator as unlikely related to mirvetuximab soravtansine.
L798: **Reviewer comment:** FDA acknowledged that palliative sedation may have preceded the patient's death but believes the underlying cause of death is more likely to be due to intestinal obstruction related to disease progression. This event was not considered as an adverse event leading to death.

L866: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L867: All patients enrolled on Study 0417 underwent a 12-lead ECG prior to the first dose of mirvetuximab; subsequent ECGs on study were not scheduled, but could have been obtained by investigators as needed. Five patients experienced TEAEs that were classified as arrhythmias (2 with sinus tachycardia, 1 supraventricular arrhythmia, 1 atrial fibrillation and 1 ECG repolarization abnormality). These events were assessed as not likely related to mirvetuximab therapy. It was determined mirvetuximab did not cause meaningful changes in ECG findings over time.
L868: ## QT
L869: QT/IRT consultation was requested for review of this initial BLA application. The QT team previously reviewed QT data collected during Study IMGN853-0401 (Study 0401), which was conducted under IND 111915. An additional QT study was not performed on Study 0417
L870: The QT data from Study 0401 revealed no large changes in mean QTc intervals (&gt;20 msec) at the recommended dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine (6 mg/kg AIBW every 3 weeks). Likewise, no increases in QTcF of &gt; 500 msec were observed in patients treated with mirvetuximab soravtansine. Central tendency analysis of the QTcF interval changes from baseline showed that the highest mean (upper bound of the 2-sided 90% CI) for QTcF was 8.1 (4.1, 13.0) msec. This level of change was observed at cycle 3/Day 1 in the Expansion cohort 3 on that study. As a result of this assessment, Section 12 of product labeling has included language to convey that, at the approved dose, mirvetuximab does not cause a large mean increase (&gt; 10 msec) in the QTc interval.
L871: *Reviewer comment: See also QT consult by Drs. Kitabi, Ruiz, and Stockbridge, dated 6/30/22.*
L872: ## Immunogenicity
L873: Potential for immunogenicity against mirvetuximab was assessed by testing of blood samples for the presence of anti-drug antibodies (ADA) at timepoints including C1D1, C2D1, C4D1, end of therapy and at 30-day follow up. These assessments were performed on 3 separate studies, including 0417, 0401, and 0403. The analysis included data from 423 patients with ovarian cancer treated across the studies. With a median duration of treatment of 4.3 months, 55/423 (13%) of ovarian cancer patients treated at the 6 mg/kg mirvetuximab dose had at least 1 post-baseline positive sample for ADA. Of these, 28/423 (7%) developed treatment-emergent ADA and 3/423 (0.7%) developed treatment enhanced ADA. Neutralizing antibodies were detected in 24/423 (6%) of patients.
L874: **Reviewer comment:** The conclusion about the immunogenicity data is that the overall occurrence of anti-mirvetuximab antibodies across the three clinical studies was relatively low. It is not known what effect these antibodies had or may have on the PK, efficacy, or safety of mirvetuximab, therefore, at this time the impact of ADA on the PK, safety, and efficacy of mirvetuximab is not fully characterized. Pertinent information on immunogenicity was included in Section 12 of product labeling.
L875: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L876: ## Ocular Adverse Events/Toxicity
L877: Ocular AEs are an adverse event of special interest with mirvetuximab. FDA conducted an independent assessment of the ocular toxicity, focusing mainly on Study 0417, but also considering data from Studies 0401 and 0403 (FORWARD1). See also Ophthalmology Consult from Dr. Wiley Chambers dated 7/8/22.
L878: Terms included in conducting the analysis of ocular toxicities included conjunctival disorders, corneal disorders, lacrimal disorders (dry eye), eye disorders (broad SOC), ocular infections, optic nerve disorders, periorbital and eyelid disorders, scleral disorders, and retinal disorders.
L879: Ocular monitoring on Study 0417 included the use of ocular examinations when symptoms were reported according to CTCAE criteria. Prophylactic use of corticosteroids and lubricating tears were practices that were a part of the study protocol and were implemented for all patients on study.
L880: An overview of the ocular toxicities on Study 0417 is shown in Table 39.
L881: The majority of patients (59%) had ocular TEAEs on Study 0417. The most common ocular adverse events were visual impairment (54%), keratopathy (37%), and dry eye (26%). The majority of ocular adverse events were of grade 1-2 in severity, but 8% of patients on Study 0417 experienced grade 3-4 visual impairment and 9% of patients experienced grade 3-4
L882: keratopathy. In addition, according to FDA analysis, 35% of patients who experienced ocular adverse events on Study 0417 did not have resolution of their ocular AEs at the last follow-up. This was the topic of multiple requests for additional information and datasets from the Applicant, due to difficulties in confirming the Applicant's proposed data on ocular adverse events and the resolution of these events.
L883: **Table 39 Study 0417 Ocular Adverse Events**

L885: **Reviewer's comment:** As noted, 59% of patients experienced ocular AEs and 35% of patients had unresolved ocular toxicities. The most common ocular AEs that were not resolved were visual impairment (22%), keratitis (17%), dry eye (12%), and cataracts (10%).
L886: One patient developed Grade 4 keratopathy on Study 0417, which led to discontinuation of mirvetuximab therapy. The narrative for this patient is described as follows:
L887: *Patient IMGN853-0417-(b) (6) developed Grade 4 keratopathy in the left eye and Grade 2 in the right eye during Cycle 2. She discontinued treatment the following cycle but continued to have blurry vision. The Applicant provided additional clinical information and ophthalmologic examinations for this patient, which were reviewed by Dr. Chambers. It was confirmed that vision loss was the reason for determining the severity as Grade 4 keratopathy. This adverse event, including the vision loss, subsequently resolved after discontinuation of the drug.*
L888: ## Ocular analysis in safety database
L889: An analysis of ocular toxicities in the pooled population of 464 patients with ovarian cancer treated on Studies 0417, 0403, and 0401 was also conducted and is shown in Table 40. Overall, the incidence of severe ocular AEs was similar between Study 0417 and in the pooled population, with 61% of patients treated with mirvetuximab experiencing an ocular adverse event. There were slight differences in the frequency of dose interruptions and reductions in the pooled database, as compared to Study 0417 alone, however the frequency of treatment discontinuations due to ocular AEs was low. According to FDA analysis, 30% of patients in the safety database had ocular AEs that were listed as not recovered by the end of follow-up.
L890: Table 40 Ocular adverse events in pooled ovarian cancer safety database
L891: Ocular AEs	MIRV
N=464 (%)

L892: ## **Reviewer comment:**
L893: *It is notable that the ocular monitoring, management, and risk mitigation strategy for patients receiving mirvetuximab evolved over the course of the development program. On the dose escalation study, 0401, there was minimal monitoring and patients were only assessed based upon reports of ocular AEs. As it became more apparent that ocular toxicities were a key adverse event in patients receiving mirvetuximab, the Applicant implemented scheduled monitoring and prophylactic measures in an attempt to improve the ocular tolerability.*
L894: *FDA assessed the use of CTCAE grading of severity of ocular toxicities, as well as the interventions for ocular AEs, based on patient-reported symptoms, rather than routine examinations. One difficulty in conducting the ocular safety review for the mirvetuximab database relates to the use of the CTCAE grading system for ocular adverse events, which relies on symptom-based reporting of AEs. The reason this system is not ideal for ocular adverse events is because there are certain ocular adverse events, such as corneal ulcers, that can be present without symptoms until the event has become severe (Grade 3, which correlates with corneal perforation). In this situation, the adverse event will not be detected until the toxicity has become significant. Additionally, certain ocular adverse events, such as cataracts, do not typically resolve on their own, even after discontinuation of the offending agent.*
L895: *Discussions took place between the Applicant and FDA, regarding the issue of the reporting of partial and complete resolution of ocular toxicities. It was ultimately agreed upon between FDA and the Applicant that out of the safety database, (n=464 patients), 61% experienced ocular adverse events. Of these patients, 49% subsequently had resolution of their ocular AEs and 39% had partial resolution, which was defined as improvement by one or more grades from worst severity grade. It is notable that although many patients reported having at least partial improvement of ocular toxicities, it could be argued that any ocular toxicity, regardless of grade, may affect quality of life, including that even Grade 2 toxicity may involve some vision loss. Given the severity and prevalence of the ocular toxicities associated with mirvetuximab therapy, the ability of the ocular toxicities to significantly affect patient functioning and quality of life,*
L896: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L897: *and the need for specialized monitoring and mitigation measures, ocular toxicity was included in product labeling as a Boxed Warning for prescribers.*
L898: *In addition, it was determined by the review team that a post-marketing requirement (PMR) was needed, mandating that the Applicant take steps to identify additional measures which may improve the ocular tolerability for patients receiving mirvetuximab. The PMR mandates that the Applicant conduct or amend an ongoing clinical trial to incorporate a prospectively specified ocular monitoring approach. The monitoring will need to employ scheduled ocular assessments of patients throughout the trial, rather than utilizing symptom-based ocular examinations only. In this trial, the Applicant will also need to investigate additional risk mitigation strategies, such as supportive care prophylaxis. It was advised, and is expected, that the Applicant will meet with FDA in the future to discuss proposed trial designs and mitigation plans as part of an approach to fulfill the post-marketing requirement.*
L899: ## Peripheral neuropathy
L900: Peripheral neuropathy was identified as another adverse event of special interest for mirvetuximab, given that the payload, DM4, is a tubulin-directed compound. Patients with &gt; Grade 1 peripheral neuropathy at baseline were excluded from Study 0417, which is relevant to the study population with ovarian cancer, given that these are patients who have received prior neurotoxic chemotherapies including taxanes and platinum.
L901: On Study 0417 (n=106), 28% of patients reported all-grade adverse events of peripheral neuropathy while on study. The analysis of these events involved pooling of terms including neurotoxicity, peripheral sensory and motor neuropathy, hypoesthesia, polyneuropathy, neuralgia, and oral hypoesthesia. The incidence of severe peripheral neuropathy on Study 0417 was 2%, including two patients experiencing Grade 3 neuropathy, one of whom discontinued treatment with mirvetuximab due to severe peripheral neuropathy.
L902: The narratives for the two patients experiencing Grade 3 neuropathy on Study 0417 are discussed as follows:
L903: *Patient 0417- [REDACTED](b)(6): Event reported as grade 3 peripheral sensory and motor neuropathy / sciatica; resulted in treatment discontinuation. This patient experienced Grade 2 motor neuropathy and Grade 3 sensory neuropathy on Day 144 of treatment and did have a past medical history of prior platinum chemotherapy and diabetes mellitus. However, the patient did not have baseline neuropathy reported and this AE was thought to be related to the study drug and led to discontinuation of the drug. She subsequently discontinued treatment for progression of disease. The end of treatment visit was on [REDACTED](b)(6) and the AEs of Grade 2 Peripheral motor neuropathy and Grade 3 Peripheral sensory neuropathy were not resolved and were assessed by the investigator as probably related to mirvetuximab soravtansine. FDA agrees with the attribution to study drug.*
L904: **Patient 0417-**(b)(6): *Event of peripheral motor neuropathy. This patient experienced Grade 3 neuropathy after 7 cycles of mirvetuximab. The patient had received chemotherapy prior to enrollment in Study 0417 but was not noted to have baseline neuropathy. The peripheral neuropathy was not resolved at the time of the patient's last assessment and she discontinued treatment one month later due to progression of disease.*
L905: In the larger safety database of 464 patients with ovarian cancer who received mirvetuximab on clinical trials, peripheral neuropathy was reported in 167 patients (36%). This included 9 patients with G3 peripheral neuropathy (2%) and 2 patients (0.4%) who discontinued mirvetuximab due to peripheral neuropathy. The median time to onset of peripheral neuropathy symptoms was 1.3 months (range 0.03 to 29.1 months). Of patients who experienced peripheral neuropathy during therapy with mirvetuximab, 28% had complete resolution and 13% had partial improvement in neuropathy symptoms. Partial improvement was defined as a decrease in severity by one or more grades from the worst grade experienced. This assessment also indicates that 98 patients (59%) in the mirvetuximab database who experienced peripheral neuropathy while receiving mirvetuximab did not have resolution (partial or complete) of these events, though majority of events were G1-2 in severity.
L906: **Reviewer comment:** *It is notable that the incidence of peripheral neuropathy in the larger safety base was higher than on the Study 0417 (36% vs. 28%), the reason for this is unclear, however, the majority of events throughout were Grade 1 or Grade 2 in severity. FDA noted that eligibility criteria in Study 0417 permitted enrollment of patients with baseline Grade 1 neuropathy. Review of the data revealed that 15 patients on the Study 0417 had Grade 1 neuropathy at study baseline. Of the patients who had adverse events of peripheral neuropathy, 7 of them had neuropathy at baseline. Neither of the 2 patients who experienced Grade 3 neuropathy on study had neuropathy at baseline, and their events were not completely resolved at data cutoff. While peripheral neuropathy is also a risk with certain chemotherapy agents and was seen at higher rates with paclitaxel in the randomized Study 0403, FDA disagrees with the Applicant's claim that the rates of neuropathy seen with Mirvetuximab are an "improvement over all chemotherapy agents and regimens available to patients in the second-line setting", as this cannot be adequately assessed in the absence of a randomized trial, comparing the agents directly. Both the pooled analysis and the individual analysis of peripheral neuropathy for Study 0417 supported the addition of peripheral neuropathy to the Warnings and Precautions section of the prescribing information for Mirvetuximab.*
L907: ## Pneumonitis
L908: Pneumonitis affected 8% of patients on Study 0417. Though there were no cases of Grade 3-4 pneumonitis events reported on Study 0417, there were four (n=4) Grade \ge 3 events in the overall safety population including 464 patients with ovarian cancer. There was one patient on Study 0417 who died due to respiratory failure in the setting of pneumonitis. The narrative was described previously in this review in Section 8.2.2 (discussion of death narratives).
L909: *Reviewer comment: Pneumonitis was included in product labeling as a Warning and Precaution in Section 5 with guidance for investigators to monitor for and manage with potential toxicity.*
L910: ## Hypersensitivity and Infusion-related reactions
L911: Hypersensitivity and infusion-related reactions (IRRs) were designated by the Applicant as AEs of Special Interest. The initial FDA review indicated the incidence of all-grade hypersensitivity reactions to be 13% on Study 0417 and the incidence of all-grade infusion-related reactions to be 5%. However, FDA found overlap with patients experiencing hypersensitivity and IRR AEs and recommending grouping these events into one category. As a result, the reported incidence of the pooled terms for all-grade hypersensitivity / infusion-related reactions was 9% on Study 0417. There was one patient who experienced a Grade 3 infusion-related reaction which was an SAE and resulted in permanent discontinuation of mirvetuximab, and the narrative is described below.
L912: *Patient 0417- [REDACTED](b)(6) - The event was reported to be a Grade 3 infusion-related reaction at Cycle 2 of treatment, which included hypotension and hypoxia. This resolved after 12 hours and was attributed to mirvetuximab soravtansine treatment. The patient discontinued treatment following this event and was discharged from the hospital the same day.*
L913: **Reviewer comment:** FDA assessment is that the majority of both toxicity events (hypersensitivity and IRR) were mild and did not lead to treatment discontinuation in most cases. Product labeling provides guidance to prescribers that all patients should receive premedication with acetaminophen, diphenhydramine, and dexamethasone prior to infusion. Likewise, dose modification and management guidelines for infusion-related and hypersensitivity reactions are included in Section 2 of product labeling.
L914: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L915: Clinical outcome assessment analyses were not included in this clinical study.

L924: **Reviewer's comments:** FDA did not note any significant safety signals specifically affecting older patients receiving mirvetuximab on Study 0417. L925: ## 8.2.8. **Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials**
L926: No specific study or clinical trial was conducted to evaluate specific safety concerns.
L927: ## 8.2.9. **Additional Safety Explorations**
L928: ## **Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development**
L929: Carcinogenicity studies were not conducted with mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx or DM4.
L930: ## **Human Reproduction and Pregnancy**
L931: Fertility studies have not been conducted with mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx or DM4. Mirvetuximab soravtansine can cause embryo-fetal harm given that it contains the genotoxic compound DM4 and it affects actively dividing cells. The pregnancy status of females of reproductive potential should be confirmed prior to initiating therapy with mirvetuximab soravtansine. Female patients of reproductive potential should be advised to use effective contraception during treatment with mirvetuximab soravtansine and for 7 months after the last dose. This information is included in Section 8 of product labeling.
L932: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L933: Safety and effectiveness of mirvetuximab soravtansine have not been established in pediatric patients.
L934: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L935: Not Applicable.
L936: ## 8.2.10. **Safety in the Postmarket Setting**
L937: ## **Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience**
L938: Not Applicable.
L939: ## **Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting**
L940: As noted in section 8.2.5.1, FDA and the Applicant had multiple discussions regarding the implementation of serial ophthalmologic assessments in patients treated with Mirvetuximab.
L941: **Reviewer's Comments:** FDA issued a Postmarket Requirement that the Applicant conduct a clinical trial, or amend existing clinical trials, of mirvetuximab soravtansine to incorporate prospectively specified, scheduled ophthalmologic assessments in all patients (symptomatic or asymptomatic) to further characterize the incidence and severity of mirvetuximab soravtansine-related ocular adverse events and evaluate additional risk mitigation strategies for ocular
L942: *adverse events. As noted in Section 8.2.7, a Postmarket Commitment was also issued to conduct analyses in patients from racial and ethnic minority groups as well.*
L943: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L944: FDA overall determined that Study 0417 adequately characterized the safety profile of mirvetuximab soravtansine in the indicated population and that the pooled safety population including additional data from Studies 0401 and 0403 were supportive of the findings on Study 0417. There were drug discontinuations due to AEs in 12% of patients in Study 0417. The main toxicities associated with mirvetuximab soravtansine were related to gastrointestinal symptoms, ocular adverse events and peripheral neuropathy. Ocular adverse events occurred in the majority of patients (59%) in 0417 and this resulted in inclusion of this toxicity as a boxed warning in the USPI proposed for the drug. FDA investigated the methods used to grade the severity of this AE by symptoms alone and did not believe that would be adequate to monitor ocular safety. Thus, FDA recommended routine ophthalmology examinations for the first 8 cycles of Mirvetuximab treatment due to the risk of asymptomatic toxicities progressing while on this therapy. In addition, peripheral neuropathy adverse events were overall low grade but affected almost one-third of patients and this toxicity was also added to the Warnings and Precautions for mirvetuximab soravtansine though FDA agrees with the Applicant that this patient population would be considering chemotherapy agents that also have risk for neuropathy. Mirvetuximab soravtansine demonstrated acceptable tolerability for the indicated population with a serious and life-threatening disease. Adverse reactions were common, but overall low-grade and manageable with adequate safety monitoring procedures.
L945: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L946: In general, there were no notable statistical issues with the trial design, statistical analysis plan, or efficacy results for the Study 0417. The initial sample size included 106 patients, however, two patients were excluded during review of the data: one for not meeting the definition of having platinum-resistant disease and one for major protocol violation, making the Efficacy Population inclusive of n=104 patients. The confirmed ORR was 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6) in the target population (n=104) with an estimated median duration of response of 5.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 7.7 months), per local investigator assessment (data cutoff: 11/16/21). At the 120-day safety update, the median DOR by investigator assessment per RECIST v1.1 increased to 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7 months) (data cutoff: 4/29/22). The efficacy evaluation was based on the magnitude of response rate and adequate duration of response.
L947: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L948: Mirvetuximab soravtansine demonstrated an ORR of 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6) and a median DOR of 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7) in patients with FR\alpha- positive, platinum-resistant ovarian cancer who have received 1-3 prior systemic regimens in Study 0417. Available therapies in this
L949: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L950: treatment setting include single agent chemotherapies with ORRs ranging from 6-27%, however, these data were collected prior to establishment of a platinum-based doublet \pm bevacizumab followed by maintenance treatment with bevacizumab, niraparib, olaparib, or olaparib + bevacizumab as 1L standard-of-care treatment for advanced ovarian cancer. The FDA review team assessed the chemotherapy control arms from recent trials in platinum-resistant ovarian cancer to determine an ORR of 12-13% and a median DOR of 4-7.4 months as a more contemporary benchmark of efficacy for available therapies. There are no available therapies specifically for treatment of FR\alpha-positive, platinum-resistant ovarian cancer. The review team also examined blinded ORR/DOR data from Study 0416 L, an ongoing randomized trial in a similar treatment setting to support efficacy results from Study 0417. Considering the results from Study 0417, supported by data from Study 0416, within the current therapeutic landscape for platinum-resistant ovarian cancer, the review team considers that mirvetuximab soravtansine demonstrates an improvement compared to available therapies and is reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit in the indicated population.
L951: Mirvetuximab soravtansine demonstrated acceptable safety and tolerability in the indicated population with a serious and life threatening condition and limited therapeutic options. The safety of mirvetuximab soravtansine is manageable through labeling, which includes a boxed warning for ocular toxicity, premedication, dose modification, and supportive care. Because mirvetuximab soravtansine was associated with high rate of ocular toxicities, a PMR will be issued to further characterize the incidence and severity of mirvetuximab soravtansine-related ocular adverse events and evaluate additional risk mitigation strategies for ocular adverse events. Also, there was limited dosage exploration for mirvetuximab soravtansine, and the product was associated with a high frequency of toxicities. It is possible that a different dosing schedule may have a more optimal benefit-risk profile for mirvetuximab soravtansine, therefore, FDA will issue a dose-finding postmarketing requirement for evaluation of alternate dosing schedules.
L952: *Based on a favorable benefit-risk assessment, FDA clinical and statistical reviewers recommend accelerated approval for the following indication:*
L953: *Mirvetuximab soravtansine for the treatment of adult patients with FR\alpha positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. Select patients for therapy based on an FDA-approved test.*
L954: *This indication is approved under accelerated approval based on tumor response rate and duration of response. Continued approval for this indication may be contingent upon verification and description of clinical benefit in a confirmatory trial.*
L955: ## 9 Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations
L956: FDA did not refer this application to an advisory committee as there were no efficacy or safety issues identified during the review which required external input for the proposed indication.
L957: ## 10 Pediatrics
L958: Included in this BLA submission was a request for waiver for study in all pediatric age based on the rationale that mirvetuximab soravtansine requires significant expression of FR\alpha on the cell membrane. The majority of FR\alpha expression in non-epithelial pediatric cancers are cytoplasmic in nature. Therefore, the planned waiver justification across all pediatric age groups is based on the fact that mirvetuximab soravtansine is unlikely to provide meaningful clinical benefit to children and is unlikely to be used.

L969: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L970: Based on the benefit-risk assessment for mirvetuximab soravtansine, safety issues can be managed through appropriate labeling and routine post-marketing surveillance. A boxed warning for ocular toxicities has also been included in product labeling. The review team determined that a REMS is not required for this application.
L971: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L972: The following PMRs and PMCs were agreed upon in association with this accelerated approval:
L973: ## 1. PMR – Accelerated Approval
L974: Conduct the clinical trial IMGN853-0416 (MIRASOL) titled “A randomized, open-label, Phase 3 study of Mirvetuximab soravtansine vs. Investigator’s choice chemotherapy in platinum-resistant, advanced, high-grade epithelial ovarian, primary peritoneal, or fallopian tube cancers with high folate receptor-alpha expression” and provide the final progression-free survival (PFS), overall response rate (ORR) and overall survival (OS) analyses to obtain data on the clinical efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine for patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer.
L975: Interim Report Submission:	09/2023
Trial Completion:	06/2024
Final Report Submission:	12/2024
L976: ## 2. PMR-FDAAA
L977: Conduct a randomized clinical trial of at least 2 dose schedules of mirvetuximab soravtansine to characterize the safety of the recommended dosage of 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W and alternative dosing schedule(s) (i.e., modified weekly dosing regimen, where lower doses are administered at a weekly frequency, instead of every 3 weeks) for the treatment of adult patients with FR\alpha- positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who have received one to three prior systemic treatment regimens. The trial should evaluate safety parameters including ocular AEs (all grade, Grade \ge 2), peripheral neuropathy (all grade, Grade \ge 2), and other adverse events of interest. The trial should evaluate efficacy parameters of the alternate dosing schedules. The trial should also include sufficient clinical pharmacokinetic sampling to analyze the exposure-response relationship for efficacy and safety.
L978: Draft Protocol Submission:	04/2023
Final Protocol Submission:	07/2023
Trial Completion:	08/2026
Final Report Submission:	02/2027
L979: Submit the datasets with the final report submission.

Collect safety and pharmacokinetic information for mirvetuximab soravtansine, unconjugated DM4, and s- methyl DM4 from the trial and utilize this information to determine the appropriate starting dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine for this population.

L1034: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1035: ## 19.4.1. Summary of Applicant's Population PK Analyses
L1036: ## Executive Summary of Population PK Analysis
L1037: **The FDA's Assessment:** In general, the applicant's population PK and exposure response analyses on efficacy and safety are considered acceptable for the purpose of supporting analyses objectives. The applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1038: **The Applicant's Position:**
L1039: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	Characterize the pharmacokinetics (PK) of mirvetuximab soravtansine (also known as IMGN853 or the antibody-
L1040: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L1041: drug conjugate (ADC)) in patients with folate receptor (FR\alpha)-positive ovarian cancer and to explore the impact of patient demographics on the variability in relevant PK Parameters.
Studies Included	IMGN853-0401 and IMGN853-0403 for model development; IMGN853-0417 for model validation.
Dose(s) Included	0.15 mg/kg to 7 mg/kg total body weight
Population Included	Ovarian cancer (female, n=537) and other (male, n=6)
Population Characteristics (Table 43)	General	Age: median 63 yr (range: 34.0-89.0), 238 (43.8%) \ge65 yr, 58 (10.7%) \ge75 yr
Weight: median 68.3 kg (range: 36.1-136)
Sex: 537 (98.9%) female
Race: 498 (91.7%) White, 15 (2.8%) Black, 15 (2.8%) Asian, 18 (2.95%) Other
Organ Impairment	Hepatic (NCI): 440 (81.0%) normal function, 77 (14.2%) mild impairment, 1 (0.2%) moderate impairment and missing 25 (4.6%).
Renal (CrCL): 197(36.3%) normal function, 217(40.0%) mild impairment, 127(23.4%) moderate impairment, and 2(0.4%) severe impairment.
Pediatrics (if any)	Not applicable
No. of Patients, PK Samples, and below level of quantitation (BLQ)	No. of patients: 543
No. of PK samples: 6819, mirvetuximab soravtansine, 3745 unconjugated DM4, and 3749 S-methyl-DM4
N (%) BLQ: 60 (0.88%) mirvetuximab soravtansine, 139 (37.3%) unconjugated DM4, and 116 (3.09%) S-methyl-DM4
Sampling Schedule	Rich or Sparse Sampling	401: Schedule A (Q3W): For Schedule A patients in Cycles 1 and 3, blood samples for PK measurements were taken at the following nominal time points: Pre-dose, immediately within 10 minutes of completion of infusion, and 2, 4, 6, 8, 24, 48, 72 or 96, 168, 336, and 504 hours post (start of) infusion. In Cycles 2, 4, 5 and 6, blood samples were collected pre-dose, and immediately after the end of infusion. Additional samples were collected at the End of Treatment and 28-Day Follow Up visits.
Schedule B (Modied QW): For Schedule B patients in Cycles 1 and 3, Days 1 and 15, blood samples were taken at the following nominal time points: Pre-dose, within 10
L1042: minutes of completion of infusion, and 2, 4, 24, and 168 hours post (start of) infusion. On Day 8 in Cycles 1 and 3, samples were collected pre-dose and post-infusion. In Cycles 2, 4, 5 and 6, blood samples were collected pre-dose, and immediately after the end of infusion. Additional samples were collected at the End of Treatment and 28-Day Follow Up visits.
403: Pre-dose and within 5 minutes following IMGN853 infusion on Day 1 of Cycles 1, 2, 3, and 4; single samples were taken on Days 8 and 15 of Cycles 1 and 3, at the End of Treatment and 30-Day Follow-up. Blood samples for immunogenicity were taken from the PK sample on Day 1 of Cycles 1, 2, and 4. Separate blood samples for assessment of ADA were collected pre-dose on Day 1 of Cycle 6 (if feasible).
417: Sparse samples were collected during the study on Cycle 1 Day 1, Cycle 1 Day 8, Cycle 2 Day 1, Cycle 3 Day 1, Cycle 3 Day 8, and Cycle 4 Day 1. On dosing days, samples were collected at pre-dose and the end of infusion; on other days only one sample was collected.

In ITT Population	NA
Covariates Evaluated	
Covariate relationships that were evaluated using the stepwise covariate modeling (SCM) method.

VDM4: AIBW, baseline tumor burden
CLS-methyl-DM4: AIBW, CRCL, ALT, ALP, AST and CYP3A inh/ind conmeds
VS-methyl-DM4: AIBW, baseline tumor burden
AIBW: Adjusted ideal body weight. ALB: Albumin. ALP: Alkaline phosphatase. ALT: Alanine aminotransferase.

Final Model	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Software and Version	NONMEM® (version 7.4)	Acceptable
Model Structure	Mirvetuximab soravtansine: a two-compartment model with combined linear (non-spec) and nonlinear (target-mediated) elimination from the central compartment
DM4 and metabolite: a central and a target compartment for DM4 and a central compartment for S-methyl-DM4	Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 44 and Table 45	Acceptable
Uncertainty and Variability (Relative Standard Error [RSE], IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	Parameters were generally estimated with good precision (RSE <20%) except for ABW on V2ADC, and AST on METDM4. (Table 44 and Table 45)	
BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	PK observations BLQ were retained in the dataset and were flagged as missing. In total, 0.792%, 0.771%, 37.2%, and 2.99% of the mirvetuximab soravtansine, total antibody (TAB), DM4, and S-methyl-DM4 observations were reported as BQL, respectively. Mirvetuximab soravtansine, DM4, and S-methyl-DM4 concentration values that were BQL were omitted from the PopPK analysis. TAB concentrations were not evaluated in the PopPK models.	Acceptable
Goodness of Fit (GOF), Visual Predictive check (VPC)	Figure 8, Figure 9, Figure 10, and Figure 11	Acceptable
Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance	Figure 12 and Figure 13	Acceptable

L1044: Labeling Language	Description	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
12.2 PK	In an exposure-response analysis, mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx exposure was associated with higher probability of therapeutic response; higher mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx exposure was associated with higher incidence of some adverse reactions (e.g., Grade \ge 2 ocular adverse reactions, Grade \ge 2 peripheral neuropathy).	We recommended as follows: An exposure-response relationship between mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx and overall response rates was observed. Higher incidence of Grade \ge 2 ocular adverse reactions and Grade \ge 2 peripheral neuropathy occurred with increasing mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx exposure.
12.3 PK	Based on population pharmacokinetic analysis, no clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx were observed for age (34 - 89 years), body weight (36 - 136 kg), mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \leULN and any AST >ULN or total bilirubin >1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST, n= 71), or mild or moderate renal impairment (CrCL \ge 30 and <90 mL/min, n= 303). The pharmacokinetics of ELAHERE in	No clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx at the proposed dosing regimen, 6mg/kg AIBW were observed for age (34 - 89 years), body weight (36 - 136 kg), mild hepatic
L1045: patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin >1.5 ULN with any AST) or severe renal impairment (CrCL <30 mL/min) is unknown.	impairment (total bilirubin \leULN and any AST >ULN or total bilirubin >1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST, n= 71), or mild or moderate renal impairment (CrCL \ge30 and <90 mL/min, n= 303). The pharmacokinetics of ELAHERE in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin >1.5 ULN with any AST) or severe renal impairment (CrCL <30 mL/min) is unknown.

L1084: ## 19.4.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1085: ## ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L1086: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1087: The Applicant's exposure response analyses on efficacy are considered acceptable for the purpose of supporting analyses objectives. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1088: ## ER (efficacy) Assessment Summary
L1089: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1090: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	Characterize exposure-efficacy relationships for ORR, DOR and PFS.
Evaluate covariate relationships to explain potential differences in efficacy or among subgroups of subjects, relative to previous efficacy results in BC
Study Included	401, 403 and 417
Endpoints	Primary: ORR
Secondary: PFS
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	Due to differences in study design, the efficacy endpoints were analyzed in two separate data pools, one for the pivotal study (IMGN853-0417, N = 97), and the other for the supporting studies IMGN853-0401 (patients meeting the IMGN853-0403 inclusion criteria, N = 36) and IMGN853-0403 (N = 242) combined.
Population Characteristics (Table 46)	General	Age: median 63.0 yr (min, max: 34.0, 89.0)
Weight: median 68.3 kg (min, max: 36.1, 136)
Sex: 6 (1.1%) male
Race: 497 (91.7%) White, 12 (2.2%) Black, 2 (0.4%) American Indian, 15 (2.8%) Asian, 16 (3.0%) Other
Pediatrics (if any)	Not applicable
Dose(s) Included	The doses of mirvetuximab soravtansine ranged from 0.15 mg/kg to 7.0 mg/kg intravenous (IV) administration based on total body weight (TBW), which is equivalent to 0.161 mg/kg to 8.71 mg/kg based on AIBW.
Exposure Metrics Explored (range)	Cycle 1 area under the concentration vs. time curve over the first 21-day treatment cycle (AUCo-d21), maximum concentration (Cmax), and trough concentration (Cthrough) for mirvetuximab soravtansine, the payload DM4, and the metabolite, S-methyl-DM4 (safety only) were used as exposure metrics.
L1091: Covariates Evaluated	Continuous Covariates: Age, AIBW, albumin, creatinine clearance (CLcr), baseline tumor burden, and soluble folate receptor \alpha (sFR-\alpha)
Categorical Covariates: Race (white vs. non-white, as the majority was white)

Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Model Structure	Primary endpoints: ORR by logistic regression model
Other endpoints: DOR and PFS by Cox regression model	Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 47	Acceptable
Model Evaluation	Figure 14, Figure 15, Figure 16, and Figure 17	Acceptable
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	ORR: none for 417 and albumin (ALB) and soluble folate receptor \alpha: (sFR-\alpha) for pooled 401 and 403
DOR: no correlation and not performed.
PFS: Alkaline phosphatase (ALP) given Ctrough for 417 and ALB, sFR-\alpha; tumor size and age for pooled 401 and 403	Acceptable
Simulation for Specific Population	The ORR model based on mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21, and the ocular toxicity model based on mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21 were used to predict the ORR response rate and ocular AE incidence rate over a wide range of exposures. Aligned with the overlaid efficacy and safety ER curves were the AUC0-d21 ranges of mirvetuximab soravtansine for doses from 3.3 mg/kg to 7 mg/kg TBW, including 6 mg/kg AIBW, that were administered in the three clinical studies. In addition, simulated AUC0-d21 range following 6 mg/kg AIBW and 6 mg/kg TBW dosing (n=1000 subjects) is illustrated.	Acceptable
L1092: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
## {Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
L1093: Visualization of E-R relationships	Figure 14, Figure 15, Figure 16, and Figure 17	Acceptable
Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	The joined analysis showed that 6 mg/kg AIBW was a dose with balanced efficacy and safety.	Acceptable
Labeling Language	Description	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
12.2 Pharmacodynamics	In an exposure-response analysis, higher mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx exposure was associated with higher probability of therapeutic response; higher mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx exposure was associated with higher incidence of some adverse reactions (e.g., Grade \ge2 ocular adverse reactions, Grade \ge2 peripheral neuropathy).	One efficacy exposure-response analyses including multiple dose levels and one including one dose level, consistently support that higher mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx exposure was associated with higher probability of therapeutic response; one combined safety exposure-response analysis supports that higher mirvetuximab soravtansine-xxxx exposure was associated with higher incidence of some adverse reactions (e.g., Grade \ge2 ocular adverse reactions, Grade \ge2 peripheral neuropathy).

L1115: ## 19.4.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
L1116: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1117: The Applicant's exposure response analyses on safety are considered acceptable for the purpose of supporting analyses objectives. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1118: ## ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L1119: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1120: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	Characterize exposure-safety relationships for the AEs listed below
Evaluate covariate relationships to explain potential differences in safety among subgroups of subjects
Studies Included	401, 403 and 417
Population Included	Ovarian cancer and other
Endpoint	Focusing on adverse events (AE) grade 2 or higher for (1) the ocular system, (2) peripheral neuropathy, and (3) pneumonitis
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	N=542
Population Characteristics (Table 46)	General	Age: median 63.0 yr (min, max: 34.0, 89.0)
Weight: median 68.3 kg (min, max: 36.1, 136)
Sex: 6 (1.1%) male
Race: 497 (91.7%) White, 12 (2.2%) Black, 2 (0.4%) American Indian, 15 (2.8%) Asian, 16 (3.0%) Other
Pediatrics (if any)	Not applicable
Dose(s) Included	0.15 mg/kg to 7 mg/kg total body weight
Exposure Metrics Explored (range)	Cycle 1 area under the concentration vs. time curve over the first 21-day treatment cycle (AUC0-d21), maximum concentration (Cmax), and trough concentration (Ctrough) for mirvetuximab soravtansine, the payload DM4, and the metabolite, S-methyl-DM4 (safety only) were used as exposure metrics.
Covariates Evaluated	Continuous Covariates: Age, AIBW, albumin, creatinine clearance (CLcr), baseline tumor burden, and soluble folate receptor \alpha (sFR-\alpha)
Categorical Covariates: Race (white vs. non-white, as the majority was white)

Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability
[FDA's comments]
L1121: Model Structure	For dichotomous safety endpoints:
logistic regression models	Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 48	Acceptable
Model Evaluation	Figure 18 and Figure 19	Acceptable
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	
Ocular AEs: mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21 and baseline tumor size as predictor variables
Peripheral neuropathy of Grade 2 or higher: mirvetuximab soravtansine Ctrough and AUC0-d21 as statistically significant covariates.
Pneumonitis (including interstitial lung disease): None.
Few patients in the lowest AUC0-d21 decile developed a Grade 2+ ocular AE, but there was a trend that the incidence rate increased with increasing mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21.
ER relationship was relatively flat, suggesting limited impact of mirvetuximab soravtansine exposure on peripheral neuropathy.

Simulation for Specific Population	The ORR model based on mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21, which was comparable to the final ORR model based on Ctrough, and the ocular toxicity model based on mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21 were used to predict the ORR response rate and ocular AE incidence rate over a wide range of exposures (See Table 18 and Figure 20 in the ER report).	Acceptable.
Visualization of E-R relationships	Figure 18 and Figure 19	Acceptable
L1122: Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	In general, both efficacy and ocular toxicity were found to increase with increasing exposure to mirvetuximab soravtansine. The covariates identified by the models overall had limited impact on efficacy and safety, and are likely not of clinical significance.	Acceptable.

L1151: ## Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
L1152: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1153: The mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21 and Ctrough showed a relationship with efficacy in terms of ORR and PFS. Mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21 was the best exposure parameter to correlate with the adverse events of the ocular system. In general, both efficacy and ocular toxicity were found to increase with increasing exposure to mirvetuximab soravtansine. The covariates identified by the models overall had limited impact on efficacy and safety, and are likely not of clinical significance.
L1154: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1155: We agree with the Applicant's position.

L1160: ## ADC (mirvetuximab soravtansine):
L1161: The bioanalytical methods for measuring mirvetuximab soravtansine plasma concentrations are shown in Table 51 and Table 52. ELISA assays were used to measure mirvetuximab soravtansine concentrations, which utilized a murine monoclonal antimaytansine antibody (\alpha-maytansine) was used for capturing mirvetuximab soravtansine, a horseradish peroxidase (HRP) conjugated donkey antihuman immunoglobulin G (IgG) (heavy and light chain) was used for the detection, and the 3,3',5,5'-tetramethylbenzidine (TMB) was used as a chromogenic substrate.

Selectivity and matrix effect	Human plasma samples fortified with 1500 ng/mL IMGN853 TAB were prepared from 10 subjects to evaluate potential matrix effects. Nine out of 10 of the fortified samples were within 20% from the theoretical concentration. Bias ranged from -14.7% to 21.8%.
Interference and specificity	Human plasma samples from 10 subjects were analyzed for matrix interference. All samples were below LLOQ (<500 ng/mL).
Hemolysis effect	There was no effect from hemolysis on the quantitation of IMGN853 TAB.
Lipemic effect	There was no effect from lipemia on the quantitation of IMGN853 TAB.
Dilution linearity and hook effect	Dilution linearity was validated using 255000 ng/mL IMGN853 TAB as 75-, 120-, and 200-fold dilutions. The observed bias ranged from -13.1% to -9.93%.
The hook effect was evaluated by analyzing a 255000 ng/mL QC sample undiluted and at 5-, 10-, 20-, 100-, and 250-fold dilutions. No apparent hook effect observed at concentrations up to 255000 ng/mL.
Bench-top/process stability	Analyte stability in thawed matrix was demonstrated in sets of low- and high-level QCs, which were thawed and remained at room temperature for 24 hours prior to analysis.
Freeze-thaw stability	Freeze-thaw stability was demonstrated using low- and high-level QC samples, which were frozen at -80^{\circ}\text{C} \pm 10^{\circ}\text{C} and thawed at room temperature during 5 cycles.
Long-term storage	Up to 1344 days at -25^{\circ}\text{C} \pm 5^{\circ}\text{C} and at -80^{\circ}\text{C} \pm 10^{\circ}\text{C}

Cminss: 95th percentile Age (77.6 years) / median Age (63 years) shows a fold change slightly above 1.00. 5th percentile Age (46.5 years) / median Age (63 years) shows a fold change slightly below 1.00.
In all plots, the points represent the median fold change, and the horizontal lines represent the 95% Confidence Interval (CI).
L1209: Figure 13 displays eight forest plots showing the impact of significant covariates (ABW and AST) on exposure parameters (AUCss and Cminss) for DM4 and s-methyl DM4. The plots compare the 95th percentile and 5th percentile values of the covariates against the median values, relative to a reference exposure (dotted vertical line at 1.00 Fold Change Relative to Parameter).
The X-axis represents the Fold Change Relative to Parameter, ranging from 0.75 to 1.50. The grey shaded area indicates the range of 0.8 to 1.25 fold change from the reference value.
The plots are grouped by covariate (ABW or AST) and parameter (AUCss or Cminss) for DM4 and s-methyl DM4. Each plot shows the median estimate (dot) and 95% Confidence Interval (horizontal line) for the ratio of exposure parameters at the 95th percentile versus the 5th percentile of the covariate values, compared to the median covariate values.
Specific comparisons shown:
ABW vs AUCss, DM4
ABW vs AUCss, sDM4
ABW vs Cmaxss, DM4
ABW vs Cmaxss, sDM4
AST vs AUCss, DM4
AST vs AUCss, sDM4
AST vs Cmaxss, DM4
AST vs Cmaxss, sDM4
The plots illustrate that the impact of ABW and AST on exposure parameters is generally minimal, with most estimates falling within the 0.8 to 1.25 fold change range.

Figure 18: Observed Ocular System Adverse Event (Grade 2 and Higher) Occurrence vs. Mirvetuximab Soravtansine AUC0-d21 with Model Predicted Probability Overlay. L1216: Figure 20 displays two panels illustrating the relationship between Mirvetuximab Soravtansine AUC0-d21 (\mu\text{mol}\cdot\text{h}/\text{L}) and clinical outcomes (Efficacy and Safety).
The X-axis for both panels is Mirvetuximab Soravtansine AUC0-d21 (\mu\text{mol}\cdot\text{h}/\text{L}), ranging from 50 to 250.
The Y-axis for the Upper Panel is Probability of Event (0.0 to 1.0).
Upper Panel (Probability of Event vs. AUC):
Objective Response Study 417 (Green line): Shows the predicted probability of Objective Response (ORR) increasing with AUC.
Ocular AEs (Grade 2+) (Red dashed line): Shows the predicted probability of Ocular Adverse Events (Grade 2+) increasing with AUC.
Predicted 6 mg/kg AIBW Exposure (IQR) (Blue shaded area): Represents the range of AUC values corresponding to the 6 mg/kg AIBW exposure, spanning approximately 100 to 150 \mu\text{mol}\cdot\text{h}/\text{L}.
Lower Panel (Box and Whisker Plots):
This panel displays box and whisker plots for various treatment regimens, showing the distribution of AUC0-d21 values.
Observed Data (Black Boxplots): Individual/Post-hoc estimate derived exposure values by treatment regimen.
Simulated Exposure Values (Blue Boxplots): Simulated exposure values by treatment regimen.
Treatment Regimens (Y-axis labels): 3.3 mg/kg TBW q3w (n=9), 5 mg/kg AIBW q3w (n=7), 5 mg/kg TBW q3w (n=11), 6 mg/kg AIBW q3w (n=477), Predicted 6 mg/kg AIBW q3w (n=1000), Predicted 6 mg/kg TBW q3w (n=1000), and 7 mg/kg TBW q3w (n=5).
The blue shaded area in the lower panel also indicates the range of AUC0-d21 values corresponding to the predicted 6 mg/kg AIBW exposure.
